Tool Definition: str_replace_based_edit_tool
description
Tool for viewing, creating and editing files
* The `view` command supports the following cases:
  - Directories: Lists files and directories up to 2 levels deep, ignoring hidden items and 
node_modules
  - Image files (.jpg, .jpeg, or .png): Displays the image visually
  - Text files: Displays numbered lines. Lines are determined from Python's `.splitlines()` method, 
which recognizes all standard line breaks. If the file contains more than 75000 characters, the output 
will be truncated.
  - Files with non-UTF-8 encoding display invalid bytes as hex escapes (e.g. `\xb5`). Binary files 
(containing NUL bytes) cannot be viewed
* The `create` command creates or overwrites text files with the content specified in the `file_text` 
parameter.
* The `str_replace` command replaces text in a file. Requires an exact, unique match of `old_str` 
(whitespace sensitive).
  - Will fail if `old_str` doesn't exist or appears multiple times
  - Omitting `new_str` deletes the matched text
  - Invalid bytes are shown as `\xNN` hex escapes in `view` output but are stored differently in the 
file, so an `old_str` containing them (or characters rendered from them) will not match. Target only 
clean text, or edit such lines via bash
* The `insert` command inserts the text `insert_text` at the line `insert_line`.

=== README.md
# Inventory Management Service
Inventory Management Service is a product catalog API built with Python 3.14, FastAPI, Pydantic v2, 
SQLAlchemy 2.0, PostgreSQL 18, and Alembic. The first release implements **Create Product**. Python 
3.14 supplies the standard-library UUIDv7 generator.
## Run Python directly on Windows
Install Python 3.14 and PostgreSQL 18, and start the local PostgreSQL service. Using `psql` or pgAdmin 
as a PostgreSQL administrator, create a development role and an empty database once:
```sql
CREATE ROLE inventra LOGIN PASSWORD 'inventra_dev';
CREATE DATABASE inventra OWNER inventra;
```
These credentials are for local development. From the project root, run the following PowerShell 
commands, adjusting `DATABASE_URL` if your local credentials or port differ:
```powershell
py -3.14 -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
$env:DATABASE_URL = 'postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m uvicorn inventory_management_service.main:app --reload
```
Open [Swagger UI](http://localhost:8000/docs) to try requests, [ReDoc](http://localhost:8000/redoc) 
for the API reference, or [OpenAPI JSON](http://localhost:8000/openapi.json) to import into an API 
client. Stop the server with Ctrl+C. Set `DATABASE_URL` again whenever starting from a new terminal.
On macOS or Linux use `python3.14 -m venv .venv`, `.venv/bin/python`, and `export DATABASE_URL=...`. 
Run migrations from the project root. Revision `0001` is an Alembic baseline for an empty database. 
Existing Flyway-managed Inventra databases require a separate adoption plan. The baseline downgrade 
drops the products table and is intended for disposable databases.
## Startup migrations and sample products
Application startup runs `alembic upgrade head` before accepting requests. Migration failures stop 
startup. Concurrent application workers serialize startup migrations with a PostgreSQL advisory lock. 
The database role must have permission to apply schema changes.
Revision `0002` executes `migrations/sql/0002_sample_products.sql`, inserting all 115 sample products 
(the original 15 plus 100 additional color variants). The SQL uses `ON CONFLICT (sku) DO NOTHING`, 
preserving existing product data. Alembic records the revision so later starts do not seed again. This 
seeds every database upgraded to head, including new databases. Downgrading to `0001` preserves seed 
rows because they may have been edited or referenced; downgrading to base drops the products table.
The catalog is also available in `data/sample-products.json`, with a standalone copy of the insert 
script at `data/sample-products.sql`. Keep the versioned migration SQL unchanged after deployment; add 
a new migration for future catalog changes. Include the `migrations` directory and `alembic.ini` when 
deploying the Python application.
To apply migrations without starting the API:
```powershell
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 8/393

--- PAGE 9 ---
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m alembic upgrade head
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m alembic current
```
## Create a product
```powershell
$body = @'
{
  "sku": " lap-dell-1 ",
  "name": "Dell XPS 15",
  "description": "15-inch business laptop",
  "category": "ELECTRONICS",
  "price": {"amount": 1499.99, "currency": "EUR"},
  "unitOfMeasure": "PIECE",
  "attributes": {"brand": "Dell"}
}
'@
$result = Invoke-WebRequest -Method Post -Uri http://localhost:8000/api/v1/products -ContentType 
application/json -Body $body
$result.StatusCode
$result.Headers.Location
$result.Content
```
- **201 Created** returns the persisted product and an absolute `Location` for the future GET 
endpoint. The body includes a UUIDv7 `id`, canonical `sku`, `status: ACTIVE`, UTC `createdAt` and 
`updatedAt`, and optimistic-locking `version: 0`.
- **400 Bad Request** covers malformed JSON, missing fields, invalid values, and unknown properties. 
Field-level errors include `errors` entries with `field` and `message`.
- **409 Conflict** includes the canonical `sku` and uses `urn:inventra:problem:duplicate-sku`. 
PostgreSQL enforces uniqueness during insert, including when requests race.
- **415 Unsupported Media Type** covers a body sent without a JSON media type. Unexpected failures 
return a generic **500**. Errors use RFC 9457 `application/problem+json`.
### Validation rules
| Field | Rules |
| --- | --- |
| `sku` | Required, 64 characters maximum before trimming. Starts with an ASCII letter or digit. 
Remaining characters can also include `.`, `_`, and `-`. Trimmed and uppercased. |
| `name`, `category` | Required, nonblank, at most 255 and 100 characters respectively. Trimmed. 
Category is free text. |
| `description` | Optional, at most 2,000 characters. Trimmed. Blank text becomes null. |
| `price.amount` | Required, nonnegative, up to 15 integer and 4 fractional digits. Accepts JSON 
numbers or plain decimal strings. The response emits an exact JSON number. Clients using binary 
floating-point should use a decimal-aware parser when full precision matters. |
| `price.currency` | Required, uppercase ISO 4217 code, validated against the installed ISO 4217 
table. |
| `unitOfMeasure` | Required. `PIECE`, `BOX`, `PACK`, `PALLET`, `GRAM`, `KILOGRAM`, `MILLILITER`, 
`LITER`, `CENTIMETER`, `METER`, `SQUARE_METER`, or `CUBIC_METER`. |
| `attributes` | Required JSON object with up to 50 entries. Use `{}` for none. Keys are nonblank, at 
most 64 characters. Values are strings of at most 255 characters. |
## Test
The API contract tests use FastAPI's in-process client and a stubbed create service. Run them from the 
project root after installing `requirements-dev.txt`.
```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -m "not postgres" -q
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 9/393

--- PAGE 10 ---
```
For the full suite, create a separate local test database once as a PostgreSQL administrator:
```sql
CREATE DATABASE inventra_test;
```
Then run the role setup script as that administrator (replace `postgres` with your administrator 
username if needed):
```powershell
psql -h localhost -U postgres -d inventra_test -f tests/init-test-db.sql
```
Run all tests against that database:
```powershell
$env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL = 
'postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_test'
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q
Remove-Item Env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL
```
The PostgreSQL tests cover Alembic upgrade, downgrade and re-upgrade, committed rows, exact prices, 
normalized duplicate rejection, and concurrent inserts. Each test migrates a fresh schema and drops 
only that schema at cleanup. The fixture requires an explicit test URL and a dedicated `inventra_test` 
database and role. The role has permission to create schemas in that database and has neither 
superuser nor CREATEDB privileges. Without the test URL, pytest reports the PostgreSQL tests as 
skipped. A configured database failure fails the tests.
## Extension points
- **GET products** can add read routes alongside POST in `inventory_management_service/main.py`, using 
the existing session dependency and model. Plan GET by UUID, pagination, filtering, and sorting. Add 
read-specific indexes through a new Alembic revision.
- **Update** can reuse the mapped version column for stale-write detection. Define the request and 
concurrency contract when implementing the route.
- **Soft delete** can use the nullable `deleted_at` column and the existing status consistency checks. 
Retain the SKU reservation and exclude deleted products from future reads.
- **Bulk create and bulk delete** can use separate request schemas and service operations. The current 
service owns the single-create transaction. Extract transaction-neutral insertion logic before 
implementing atomic batches.
The only registered product operation is POST. The project separates HTTP handling and Pydantic 
schemas from the create transaction and ORM model.

def postgres_text(value: str) -> str:
    if "\0" in value or re.search(r"[\ud800-\udfff]", value):
        raise ValueError("must contain valid Unicode text without NUL")
    return value

def nonblank(value: str) -> str:
    if not value.strip():
        raise ValueError("must not be blank")
    return value
Text = Annotated[StrictStr, AfterValidator(postgres_text)]
Nonblank = Annotated[Text, AfterValidator(nonblank)]
AttributeKey = Annotated[Nonblank, Field(max_length=64)]
AttributeValue = Annotated[Text, Field(max_length=255)]

class Price(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    amount: Decimal = Field(ge=0, max_digits=19, decimal_places=4, allow_inf_nan=False)
    currency: StrictStr = Field(pattern=r"^[A-Z]{3}$")
    @field_validator("amount", mode="before")
    @classmethod
    def decimal_input(cls, value):
        if type(value) not in (int, Decimal, str):
            raise ValueError("must be a decimal amount")
        if isinstance(value, str) and not re.fullmatch(r"[+-]?[0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)?", value):
            raise ValueError("must be a decimal amount")
        return value
    @field_validator("amount")
    @classmethod
    def raw_scale(cls, value: Decimal) -> Decimal:
        if value.as_tuple().exponent < -4:
            raise ValueError("must have at most 4 fractional digits")
        return value
    @field_validator("currency")
    @classmethod
    def iso_currency(cls, value: str) -> str:
        Currency(value)
        return value
class CreateProduct(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", loc_by_alias=True)

=== inventory_management_service/service.py
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from uuid import uuid7
from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
from inventory_management_service.models import Product
from inventory_management_service.schemas import CreateProduct
class DuplicateSku(Exception):
    def __init__(self, sku: str):
        self.sku = sku
def create_product(session: Session, command: CreateProduct) -> Product:
    timestamp = datetime.now(UTC)
    product = Product(
        id=uuid7(),
        sku=command.sku,
        name=command.name.strip(),
        description=(command.description or "").strip() or None,
        category=command.category.strip(),
        price_amount=command.price.amount,
        price_currency=command.price.currency,
        unit_of_measure=command.unit_of_measure.value,
        attributes=command.attributes,
        status="ACTIVE",
        created_at=timestamp,
        updated_at=timestamp,
    )
    try:
        with session.begin():
            session.add(product)
    except IntegrityError as error:
        original = error.orig

        raise
    return product
=== migrations/env.py
import os
from alembic import context
from sqlalchemy import create_engine, pool
def migrate(connection):
    context.configure(connection=connection)
    with context.begin_transaction():
        context.run_migrations()
if context.is_offline_mode():
    context.configure(dialect_name="postgresql", literal_binds=True)
    with context.begin_transaction():
        context.run_migrations()
elif (connection := context.config.attributes.get("connection")) is not None:
    migrate(connection)
else:
    engine = create_engine(os.environ["DATABASE_URL"], poolclass=pool.NullPool)
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        migrate(connection)
    engine.dispose()

from inventory_management_service.db import get_session

@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app):
    await run_in_threadpool(run_migrations)
    yield
app = FastAPI(title="Inventra Inventory Management", version="0.1.0", lifespan=lifespan)
PRODUCTS = "/api/v1/products"
PROBLEM_MEDIA = "application/problem+json"
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
def problem(request, status, detail, *, kind=None, title=None, headers=None, **extensions):
    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "type": f"urn:inventra:problem:{kind}" if kind else "about:blank",
            "title": title or HTTPStatus(status).phrase,
            "status": status,
            "detail": detail,
            "instance": request.url.path,
            **extensions,
        },
        status_code=status,
        media_type=PROBLEM_MEDIA,
        headers=headers,
    )
class InvalidBody(Exception):
    def __init__(self, errors=None, malformed=True):
        self.errors = errors or []
        self.malformed = malformed
@app.exception_handler(InvalidBody)
async def invalid_body(request, error):
    extensions = {"errors": error.errors} if error.errors else {}
    if error.malformed:
        return problem(
            request, 400, "The request body is missing or has invalid JSON fields or types.",
            kind="malformed-request", title="Malformed request body", **extensions,
        )
    return problem(
        request, 400, f"The request contains {len(error.errors)} invalid field(s).",
        kind="validation-error", title="Validation failed", **extensions,
    )
@app.exception_handler(DuplicateSku)
async def duplicate(request, error):
    return problem(
        request, 409, f"A product with SKU '{error.sku}' already exists.",
        kind="duplicate-sku", title="Duplicate SKU", sku=error.sku,
    )

@app.exception_handler(HTTPException)
async def http_error(request, error):
    return problem(request, error.status_code, error.detail, headers=error.headers)
@app.exception_handler(Exception)
async def unexpected(request, error):
    logger.error("Request failed: %s %s", request.method, request.url.path, exc_info=error)
    return problem(request, 500, "An unexpected error occurred.")
def reject_constant(token):
    raise ValueError("Non-finite JSON number")
async def parse_product(request: Request) -> CreateProduct:
    body = await request.body()
    media = request.headers.get("content-type", "").partition(";")[0].strip().lower()
    if body and media != "application/json" and not (
        media.startswith("application/") and media.endswith("+json")
    ):
        raise HTTPException(415, "Send the product as JSON.")
    try:
        payload = json.loads(body, parse_float=Decimal, parse_constant=reject_constant)
    except (ValueError, RecursionError, InvalidOperation) as error:
        raise InvalidBody() from error
    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
        raise InvalidBody()
    try:
        return CreateProduct.model_validate(payload)
    except ValidationError as error:
        errors = error.errors(include_input=False, include_context=False, include_url=False)
        violations = [
            {
                "field": ".".join(str(part) for part in item["loc"]),
                "message": item["msg"].removeprefix("Value error, "),
            }
            for item in errors
        ]
        # Invalid input can contain lone UTF-16 surrogates in property names.
        for item in violations:
            item["field"] = item["field"].encode("utf-8", "replace").decode("utf-8")
        violations.sort(key=lambda item: (item["field"], item["message"]))
        malformed = any(
            item["type"] == "extra_forbidden" or item["type"].endswith("_type")
            for item in errors
        )
        raise InvalidBody(violations, malformed) from error

}

    PRODUCTS,

def post_product(
    command: Annotated[CreateProduct, Depends(parse_product)],
    session: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_session)],
    request: Request,
):
    product = create_product(session, command)
    result = ProductResponse(
        **command.model_dump(by_alias=True, exclude={"name", "description", "category"}),
        id=product.id,
        name=product.name,
        description=product.description,
        category=product.category,

        createdAt=product.created_at,
        updatedAt=product.updated_at,
        version=product.version,
    )
    data = result.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)
    # Keep the decimal exact and emit a JSON number, as in the original API.
    data["price"]["amount"] = Decimal(format(product.price_amount.normalize(), "f"))
    base = str(request.base_url).rstrip("/")
    return Response(

        status_code=201,
        media_type="application/json",
        headers={"Location": f"{base}{PRODUCTS}/{product.id}"},
    )
default_openapi = app.openapi
def openapi():
    schema = default_openapi()
    components = schema["components"]["schemas"]
    for name, definition in request_definitions.items():
        # Price has a distinct response representation. Unit is shared.
        components.setdefault(name, definition)
    components["ProductResponse"]["properties"]["price"] = {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/PriceResponse"
    }
    components["PriceResponse"] = {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["amount", "currency"],
        "properties": {
            "amount": {"type": "number", "minimum": 0},
            "currency": {"type": "string", "pattern": "^[A-Z]{3}$"},
        },
    }
    components["Price"] = request_definitions["Price"]
    return schema
app.openapi = openapi
=== inventory_management_service/migration.py
from pathlib import Path
from alembic import command
from alembic.config import Config
from sqlalchemy import text
from inventory_management_service.db import engine
def run_migrations():
    """Apply pending migrations before serving requests, one process at a time."""
    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    config = Config(str(root / "alembic.ini"))
    with engine().begin() as connection:
        # Concurrent application workers must not migrate the database together.
        connection.execute(text("SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(746238190517)"))
        config.attributes["connection"] = connection
        command.upgrade(config, "head")

=== inventory_management_service/models.py
from datetime import datetime
from decimal import Decimal
from uuid import UUID
from sqlalchemy import BigInteger, DateTime, Numeric, String
from sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql import JSONB
from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column
from inventory_management_service.db import Base
class Product(Base):
    __tablename__ = "products"
    id: Mapped[UUID] = mapped_column(primary_key=True)
    sku: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64))
    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(255))
    description: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(2000))
    category: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(100))
    price_amount: Mapped[Decimal] = mapped_column(Numeric(19, 4))
    price_currency: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(3))
    unit_of_measure: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32))
    attributes: Mapped[dict[str, str]] = mapped_column(JSONB)
    status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16))
    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
    deleted_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
    version: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(BigInteger)
    __mapper_args__ = {
        "version_id_col": version,
        "version_id_generator": lambda current: 0 if current is None else current + 1,
    }

def postgres_text(value: str) -> str:
    if "\0" in value or re.search(r"[\ud800-\udfff]", value):
        raise ValueError("must contain valid Unicode text without NUL")
    return value
def nonblank(value: str) -> str:

def database():
    url = os.environ.get("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL")
    if not url:
        pytest.skip("Set INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL to run PostgreSQL integration tests")
    url = make_url(url)
    if (
        url.drivername != "postgresql+psycopg"
        or url.database != "inventra_test"
        or url.username != "inventra_test"
    ):
        pytest.fail("Use a dedicated PostgreSQL database and role both named inventra_test")
    schema = f"test_{uuid4().hex}"
    admin = create_engine(url)
    engine = create_engine(url, connect_args={"options": f"-c search_path={schema}"})
    try:
        with admin.begin() as connection:
            privileged = connection.scalar(text("""
                SELECT rolsuper OR rolcreatedb FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user
            """))
            if privileged:
                pytest.fail("The test role must be NOCREATEDB and NOSUPERUSER")
            connection.execute(text(f'CREATE SCHEMA "{schema}"'))
        try:
            config = Config(str(ROOT / "alembic.ini"))
            with engine.connect() as connection:
                config.attributes["connection"] = connection
                command.upgrade(config, "head")
            yield engine, config
        finally:
            engine.dispose()
            with admin.begin() as connection:
                connection.execute(text(f'DROP SCHEMA "{schema}" CASCADE'))
    finally:
        admin.dispose()

def test_create_contract(client, payload, monkeypatch):
    now = datetime.now(UTC)
    product_id = uuid7()
    def create(session, request):
        assert request.sku == "LAP-DELL-1"
        assert request.price.amount == Decimal("999999999999999.9999")
        return Product(
            id=product_id, name="Dell XPS", description=None, category="ELECTRONICS",
            status="ACTIVE", created_at=now, updated_at=now, version=0,
            price_amount=request.price.amount,
        )
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.create_product", create)
    raw = json.dumps(payload).replace('"999999999999999.9999"', "999999999999999.9999")
    response = client.post(PRODUCTS, content=raw, headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    assert UUID(data["id"]).version == 7
    assert response.headers["location"] == f"http://testserver{PRODUCTS}/{product_id}"
    assert data["price"]["amount"] == Decimal("999999999999999.9999")
    assert data["unitOfMeasure"] == "PIECE"
    assert data["status"] == "ACTIVE"
    assert data["version"] == 0
    assert data["createdAt"] == data["updatedAt"]
    assert data["description"] is None
@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", ["", "{", "null", "[]", '{"sku":NaN}'])
def test_malformed(client, raw):
    response = client.post(PRODUCTS, content=raw, headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    assert response.status_code == 400
    assert response.headers["content-type"] == "application/problem+json"
    assert response.json()["type"] == "urn:inventra:problem:malformed-request"
    assert response.json()["instance"] == PRODUCTS

def test_persistence_and_duplicate(database, client, payload):
    engine, _ = database
    first = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert first.status_code == 201, first.text
    data = first.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    duplicate = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload | {"sku": "LAP-DELL-1", "name": "Replacement"})
    assert duplicate.status_code == 409, duplicate.text
    assert duplicate.headers["content-type"] == "application/problem+json"
    assert duplicate.json()["type"] == "urn:inventra:problem:duplicate-sku"
    assert duplicate.json()["sku"] == "LAP-DELL-1"
    with Session(engine) as session:
        product = session.scalars(select(Product).where(Product.sku == "LAP-DELL-1")).one()
        assert product.id == UUID(data["id"])
        assert product.name == "Dell XPS"
        assert product.price_amount == data["price"]["amount"]
        assert product.attributes == {"brand": "Dell"}
        assert product.version == data["version"] == 0
        assert product.created_at == product.updated_at
        assert product.deleted_at is None
    assert client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload | {"sku": "new-sku"}).status_code == 201
def test_concurrent_create_has_one_winner(database, client, payload):
    engine, _ = database
    barrier = Barrier(2)
    def create(sku):
        barrier.wait(timeout=10)
        return client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload | {"sku": sku})
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as executor:
        responses = list(executor.map(create, ["lap-dell-1", " LAP-DELL-1 "]))
    assert sorted(response.status_code for response in responses) == [201, 409]
    with Session(engine) as session:
        assert len(session.scalars(select(Product).where(Product.sku == "LAP-DELL-1")).all()) == 1
def test_migration_roundtrip(database):
    engine, config = database
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        config.attributes["connection"] = connection
        assert connection.scalar(text("SELECT version_num FROM alembic_version")) == "0002"
        connection.rollback()
        command.downgrade(config, "base")
        assert not inspect(connection).has_table("products")
        connection.rollback()
        command.upgrade(config, "head")
        assert inspect(connection).has_table("products")

import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from inventory_management_service.main import app
def test_startup_runs_migrations_before_serving(monkeypatch):
    calls = []
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.run_migrations", lambda: 
calls.append("migrated"))
    with TestClient(app) as client:

def test_startup_stops_if_migration_fails(monkeypatch):
    def fail():
        raise RuntimeError("Database unavailable")
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.run_migrations", fail)
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="Database unavailable"):
        with TestClient(app):
            pytest.fail("The application must not start after a migration failure")

from datetime import datetime, UTC, timezone, timedelta
from decimal import Decimal
from typing import Annotated
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, TypeAdapter
from fastapi import FastAPI, Query
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
print(TypeAdapter(datetime).dump_python(datetime(2025,1,1,tzinfo=UTC), mode="json"))
print(TypeAdapter(datetime).dump_python(datetime(2025,1,1,0,0,0,5,tzinfo=timezone(timedelta(hours=2)))
.astimezone(UTC), mode="json"))
class Q(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", populate_by_name=False)
    page: int = Field(1, ge=1)
    min_price: Decimal | None = Field(None, alias="minPrice", ge=0, max_digits=19, decimal_places=4, 
allow_inf_nan=False)
app = FastAPI()
@app.get("/x")
def x(q: Annotated[Q, Query()]):
    return q.model_dump()
c = TestClient(app)
for qs in ["", "minPrice=1.5", "min_price=3", "foo=1", "minPrice=NaN", "minPrice=1e3", 
"minPrice=1.00001"]:
    r = c.get("/x?"+qs); print(qs, r.status_code, r.json())
print([p["name"] for p in c.get("/openapi.json").json()["paths"]["/x"]["get"]["parameters"]])
from sqlalchemy import column, String
from sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql
print(column("name", String).icontains("50%_a/b", 
autoescape=True).compile(dialect=postgresql.dialect(), compile_kwargs={"literal_binds": True}))

--- PAGE 39 ---
ASSISTANT
All behaviours confirmed. Design decisions before writing code:
- **`GET /api/v1/products/{productId}`**: 200 with the exact POST response shape; 404 problem 
(`urn:inventra:problem:product-not-found`) for both unknown and soft-deleted products, so deleted products are 
indistinguishable from missing ones. A malformed UUID returns a 400 `validation-error` problem, not FastAPI's 
default 422.
- **`GET /api/v1/products`**: `page` (1-based), `size` (default 20, max 100), `search`, `category`, 
`minPrice`/`maxPrice`, `currency`, `sort`, `direction`. Unknown query parameters are rejected with 400, matching 
how the create endpoint rejects unknown body properties.
- **Stable sorting**: every sort appends `id` as a unique tiebreaker. This matters here because all 115 seeded rows 
share the same `created_at`.
- The count and the page query run in one REPEATABLE READ snapshot, so totals match the returned items.
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("inventory_management_service/schemas.py")
s = p.read_text()
old = '''class ProductResponse(CreateProduct):
    id: UUID
    status: Literal["ACTIVE"]
'''
new = '''class ProductResponse(CreateProduct):
    id: UUID
    # Create always returns ACTIVE. Reads can also return the other non-deleted
    # statuses allowed by ck_products_status; DELETED products are never returned.
    status: Literal["ACTIVE", "INACTIVE", "DISCONTINUED"]
'''
assert old in s
s = s.replace(old, new)
s += '''
class ProductSort(StrEnum):
    NAME = "name"
    SKU = "sku"
    CATEGORY = "category"
    PRICE = "price"
    CREATED_AT = "createdAt"
    UPDATED_AT = "updatedAt"
class SortDirection(StrEnum):
    ASC = "asc"
    DESC = "desc"
def blank_to_none(value: str | None) -> str | None:
    return (value or "").strip() or None
Amount = Annotated[Decimal, Field(ge=0, max_digits=19, decimal_places=4, allow_inf_nan=False)]
class ProductQuery(BaseModel):
    """Query parameters of the product list. Unknown parameters are rejected."""

    search: Annotated[Text | None, AfterValidator(blank_to_none)] = Field(
        None,
        max_length=255,
        description=(
            "Case-insensitive search. Every whitespace-separated term must occur in the "
            "SKU, name, or description. Blank means no filter."
        ),
    )
    category: Annotated[Text | None, AfterValidator(blank_to_none)] = Field(
        None, max_length=100, description="Exact category, case-insensitive. Blank means no filter.",
    )
    min_price: Amount | None = Field(None, alias="minPrice", description="Inclusive lower bound on 
price.amount.")
    max_price: Amount | None = Field(None, alias="maxPrice", description="Inclusive upper bound on 
price.amount.")
    currency: StrictStr | None = Field(
        None,
        pattern=r"^[A-Z]{3}$",
        description="ISO 4217 price currency. Price bounds compare amounts without conversion.",
    )
    sort: ProductSort = Field(ProductSort.NAME, description="Sort field. Ties are broken by id.")
    direction: SortDirection = Field(SortDirection.ASC, description="Sort direction.")
    @field_validator("currency")
    @classmethod
    def iso_currency(cls, value: str | None) -> str | None:
        if value is not None:
            Currency(value)
        return value
class ProductPage(BaseModel):
    items: list[ProductResponse]
    page: int
    size: int
    total_items: int = Field(alias="totalItems")
    total_pages: int = Field(alias="totalPages")
'''

    def __init__(self, sku: str):
        self.sku = sku
class ProductNotFound(Exception):
    def __init__(self, product_id: UUID):
        self.product_id = product_id
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProductSlice:
    items: list[Product]
    total: int
SORT_COLUMNS = {
    ProductSort.NAME: Product.name,
    ProductSort.SKU: Product.sku,
    ProductSort.CATEGORY: Product.category,
    ProductSort.PRICE: Product.price_amount,
    ProductSort.CREATED_AT: Product.created_at,
    ProductSort.UPDATED_AT: Product.updated_at,
}
''')
s += '''
def get_product(session: Session, product_id: UUID) -> Product:
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 41/393

--- PAGE 42 ---
    with session.begin():
        product = session.scalar(
            select(Product).where(Product.id == product_id, Product.deleted_at.is_(None))
        )
    if product is None:
        # Soft-deleted products are indistinguishable from missing ones.
        raise ProductNotFound(product_id)
    return product
def list_products(session: Session, query: ProductQuery) -> ProductSlice:
    conditions = [Product.deleted_at.is_(None)]
    for term in (query.search or "").split():
        conditions.append(or_(
            Product.sku.icontains(term, autoescape=True),
            Product.name.icontains(term, autoescape=True),
            Product.description.icontains(term, autoescape=True),
        ))
    if query.category is not None:
        conditions.append(func.lower(Product.category) == func.lower(query.category))
    if query.min_price is not None:
        conditions.append(Product.price_amount >= query.min_price)
    if query.max_price is not None:
        conditions.append(Product.price_amount <= query.max_price)
    if query.currency is not None:
        conditions.append(Product.price_currency == query.currency)
    column = SORT_COLUMNS[query.sort]
    # id is unique, so the order is total and pages neither overlap nor skip rows.
    if query.direction is SortDirection.DESC:
        order = (column.desc(), Product.id.desc())
    else:
        order = (column.asc(), Product.id.asc())
    offset = (query.page - 1) * query.size
    with session.begin():
        # One snapshot for the count and the page keeps the totals consistent.
        session.connection(execution_options={"isolation_level": "REPEATABLE READ"})
        total = session.scalar(select(func.count()).select_from(Product).where(*conditions))
        items = []
        if offset < total:
            items = list(session.scalars(
                select(Product).where(*conditions).order_by(*order).offset(offset).limit(query.size)
            ))
    return ProductSlice(items, total)
'''

--- PAGE 56 ---
ASSISTANT
Two OpenAPI differences on the create operation. The `status` enum widening is intentional: reads can return the 
other non-deleted statuses the DB allows, and with `Literal["ACTIVE"]` an INACTIVE row would have caused a 500. 
The extra `productId` property in POST's problem schema is just noise, so I'll make the extension properties per-
operation and keep POST's error docs byte-identical:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("inventory_management_service/main.py")
s = p.read_text()
def rep(old, new):
    global s
    assert s.count(old) == 1, old
    s = s.replace(old, new)
rep('''def problem_docs(descriptions):
    return {''', '''def problem_docs(descriptions, **extensions):
    return {''')
rep('''                            "instance": {"type": "string"},
                            "sku": {"type": "string"},
                            "productId": {"type": "string", "format": "uuid"},
''', '''                            "instance": {"type": "string"},
                            **extensions,
''')
rep('''problem_responses = problem_docs({
    400: "Malformed body or validation failure",
    409: "Duplicate normalized SKU",
    415: "Unsupported media type",
    500: "Unexpected server error",
})''', '''problem_responses = problem_docs({
    400: "Malformed body or validation failure",
    409: "Duplicate normalized SKU",
    415: "Unsupported media type",
    500: "Unexpected server error",
}, sku={"type": "string"})''')
rep('''        404: "Product does not exist or is deleted",
        500: "Unexpected server error",
    }),''', '''        404: "Product does not exist or is deleted",
        500: "Unexpected server error",
    }, productId={"type": "string", "format": "uuid"}),''')
p.write_text(s)
EOF
export PYTHONPATH=$PWD; .venv/bin/python /tmp/dump_openapi.py /tmp/new.json; diff /tmp/old.json 
/tmp/new.json

def get_all(client, **params):
    """Walk every page of a listing and return the items plus the first page envelope."""
    first = client.get(PRODUCTS, params=params | {"page": 1})
    assert first.status_code == 200, first.text
    envelope = first.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    items = list(envelope["items"])
    for page in range(2, envelope["totalPages"] + 1):
        response = client.get(PRODUCTS, params=params | {"page": page})
        assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
        assert response.json()["totalItems"] == envelope["totalItems"]
        items += response.json(parse_float=Decimal)["items"]
    return items, envelope
def skus(items):
    return [item["sku"] for item in items]
def matching_samples(search="", category=None, low=None, high=None):
    def matches(product):
        text_ = " ".join([product["sku"], product["name"], product.get("description") or ""]).lower()
        return (
            all(term.lower() in text_ for term in search.split())
            and (category is None or product["category"].lower() == category.lower())
            and (low is None or product["price"]["amount"] >= low)
            and (high is None or product["price"]["amount"] <= high)
        )
    return {product["sku"] for product in SAMPLES if matches(product)}

def test_get_by_location_returns_created_product(client, payload):
    created = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert created.status_code == 201, created.text
    fetched = client.get(created.headers["location"])
    assert fetched.status_code == 200, fetched.text
    # Stored values, including the 19-digit price and microsecond timestamps, round-trip exactly.
    assert fetched.json(parse_float=Decimal) == created.json(parse_float=Decimal)
def test_get_seeded_product(database, client):
    engine, _ = database
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        product_id = connection.scalar(text("SELECT id FROM products WHERE sku = 'DEMO-MONITOR-001'"))
    response = client.get(f"{PRODUCTS}/{product_id}")
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    source = next(product for product in SAMPLES if product["sku"] == "DEMO-MONITOR-001")
    assert data["id"] == str(product_id)
    assert data["price"] == source["price"]
    assert '"amount": 249,' in response.text
    assert data["attributes"] == source["attributes"]
    assert (data["status"], data["version"]) == ("ACTIVE", 0)
    assert client.get(f"{PRODUCTS}/{UUID(int=0)}").status_code == 404
def test_soft_deleted_products_are_hidden_and_preserved(database, client):
    engine, _ = database
    with engine.begin() as connection:
        deleted_id = connection.scalar(text("""
            UPDATE products SET status = 'DELETED', deleted_at = created_at
            WHERE sku = 'DEMO-LAPTOP-001' RETURNING id
        """))
        inactive_id = connection.scalar(text("""
            UPDATE products SET status = 'INACTIVE' WHERE sku = 'DEMO-MOUSE-001' RETURNING id
        """))
    missing = client.get(f"{PRODUCTS}/{deleted_id}")
    assert missing.status_code == 404
    assert missing.json()["type"] == "urn:inventra:problem:product-not-found"
    assert client.get(PRODUCTS, params={"search": "DEMO-LAPTOP-001"}).json()["totalItems"] == 0
    items, envelope = get_all(client, size=100)
    assert envelope["totalItems"] == len(items) == 114
    assert "DEMO-LAPTOP-001" not in skus(items)
    # Soft deletion only hides the row. Other non-deleted statuses remain visible.
    inactive = client.get(f"{PRODUCTS}/{inactive_id}")
    assert inactive.status_code == 200, inactive.text
    assert inactive.json()["status"] == "INACTIVE"
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        assert connection.scalar(text("SELECT count(*) FROM products")) == 115
    # The SKU stays reserved by the deleted row.
    assert client.post(PRODUCTS, json={
        "sku": "demo-laptop-001", "name": "Replacement", "category": "ELECTRONICS",
        "price": {"amount": 1, "currency": "EUR"}, "unitOfMeasure": "PIECE", "attributes": {},
    }).status_code == 409

    "name": "name", "sku": "sku", "category": "category", "price": "price_amount",

@pytest.mark.parametrize("direction", ["asc", "desc"])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("sort", list(SORT_SQL))
def test_pagination_is_stable_for_every_sort(database, client, sort, direction):
    engine, _ = database
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        # Every seeded row shares one created_at, so the tiebreaker decides the createdAt order.
        assert connection.scalar(text("SELECT count(DISTINCT created_at) FROM products")) == 1
        before = connection.execute(text("SELECT * FROM products ORDER BY id")).all()
        column = SORT_SQL[sort]
        expected = [row.sku for row in connection.execute(text(
            f"SELECT sku FROM products ORDER BY {column} {direction}, id {direction}"
        ))]
    items, envelope = get_all(client, size=7, sort=sort, direction=direction)
    assert (envelope["totalItems"], envelope["totalPages"]) == (115, 17)
    assert skus(items) == expected
    assert len(set(skus(items))) == 115
    again, _ = get_all(client, size=7, sort=sort, direction=direction)
    assert skus(again) == expected
    past_end = client.get(PRODUCTS, params={"size": 7, "page": 18, "sort": sort}).json()
    assert (past_end["items"], past_end["totalItems"]) == ([], 115)
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        assert connection.execute(text("SELECT * FROM products ORDER BY id")).all() == before
def test_price_and_created_at_sorting_semantics(client):
    items, _ = get_all(client, size=50, sort="price", direction="desc")
    keys = [(item["price"]["amount"], UUID(item["id"])) for item in items]
    assert keys == sorted(keys, reverse=True)
    assert keys[0][0] == max(product["price"]["amount"] for product in SAMPLES)
    items, _ = get_all(client, size=50, sort="createdAt")
    assert [UUID(item["id"]) for item in items] == sorted(UUID(item["id"]) for item in items)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("category", ["ELECTRONICS", "electronics", " Office_Supplies ", "KITCHEN", 
"UNKNOWN"])
def test_category_filter(client, category):
    items, envelope = get_all(client, category=category, size=100)
    expected = matching_samples(category=category.strip())
    assert set(skus(items)) == expected
    assert envelope["totalItems"] == len(expected)
    assert {item["category"] for item in items} <= {category.strip().upper()}
@pytest.mark.parametrize(("low", "high"), [
    ("24.9", "24.90"), ("10", "50"), ("0", "9.99"), ("500", None), (None, "5"), ("1000000", None),
])
def test_price_filters_are_inclusive(client, low, high):
    params = {key: value for key, value in {"minPrice": low, "maxPrice": high}.items() if value is not 
None}
    items, envelope = get_all(client, size=100, **params)
    expected = matching_samples(low=low and Decimal(low), high=high and Decimal(high))
    assert set(skus(items)) == expected
    assert envelope["totalItems"] == len(expected)
    if low == high == "24.9":
        assert "DEMO-MOUSE-001" in expected
def test_combined_filters_and_sort(client):
    params = {"search": "black", "category": "electronics", "minPrice": "20", "maxPrice": "100",
              "sort": "price", "direction": "desc"}
    items, envelope = get_all(client, size=2, **params)
    expected = matching_samples("black", "ELECTRONICS", Decimal(20), Decimal(100))
    assert expected and set(skus(items)) == expected
    assert envelope["totalItems"] == len(expected)
    prices = [item["price"]["amount"] for item in items]
    assert prices == sorted(prices, reverse=True)

rep('''## Test
''', '''## Get a product
`GET /api/v1/products/{productId}` returns the product with the same representation as the create 
response: exact JSON-number price, canonical SKU, UTC timestamps, and `version`. `status` can be 
`ACTIVE`, `INACTIVE`, or `DISCONTINUED`; create always returns `ACTIVE`.
```bash
B=http://localhost:8000/api/v1/products
# A seeded product (sample IDs are fixed by the seed migration).
curl -s "$B/01a1b052-ec7c-76db-a862-e0da4baa5110"
# Follow the Location header of a create request.
LOCATION=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{redirect_url}' -X POST "$B" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' 
\\
  -d '{"sku":"lap-dell-1","name":"Dell XPS 15","category":"ELECTRONICS","price":
{"amount":1499.99,"currency":"EUR"},"unitOfMeasure":"PIECE","attributes":{}}')
curl -s "$LOCATION"
```
```powershell
Invoke-RestMethod http://localhost:8000/api/v1/products/01a1b052-ec7c-76db-a862-e0da4baa5110
```
- **200 OK** returns the product.
- **404 Not Found** uses `urn:inventra:problem:product-not-found` and includes `productId`. Soft-
deleted products (`deleted_at` set, status `DELETED`) return the same 404 as products that never 
existed.
- **400 Bad Request** uses `urn:inventra:problem:validation-error` when the ID is not a UUID, with an 
`errors` entry for `productId`.
## List products
`GET /api/v1/products` returns one page of products that are not soft-deleted:
```json
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 76/393

--- PAGE 77 ---
{"items": [{"sku": "DEMO-MONITOR-001", "...": "..."}], "page": 1, "size": 20, "totalItems": 115, 
"totalPages": 6}
```
`items` use the Get Product representation. A page past the end returns `200` with empty `items` and 
the real totals. The count and the page are read from one snapshot, so they agree even while products 
are being created.
| Parameter | Default | Rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `page` | `1` | 1-based page number, 1 to 2,147,483,647. |
| `size` | `20` | Products per page, 1 to 100. |
| `search` | none | Case-insensitive. Every whitespace-separated term must occur in the SKU, name, or 
description, so `ring binder blue` finds `A4 Ring Binder - Blue`. `%`, `_` and `\\` match literally. 
At most 255 characters. Blank means no filter. |
| `category` | none | Exact category, case-insensitive and trimmed, so `electronics` matches 
`ELECTRONICS`. Blank means no filter. |
| `minPrice`, `maxPrice` | none | Inclusive bounds on `price.amount`, nonnegative, up to 15 integer 
and 4 fractional digits. `minPrice` must not exceed `maxPrice`. |
| `currency` | none | Uppercase ISO 4217 code. Price bounds compare amounts without currency 
conversion, so combine them with `currency` when the catalog has several currencies. |
| `sort` | `name` | `name`, `sku`, `category`, `price`, `createdAt`, or `updatedAt`. |
| `direction` | `asc` | `asc` or `desc`. |
Sorting is stable: ties are always broken by product `id` in the same direction, so paging through a 
result never repeats or skips a product while the data is unchanged. This matters for the sample 
catalog because all 115 seeded products share one `createdAt`. Text sorting follows the database 
collation. Offset paging can shift by a row if products are created or deleted between page requests.
Filters combine with AND. Unknown parameters, such as a misspelled `categroy`, are rejected instead of 
being ignored. Invalid parameters return **400** `urn:inventra:problem:validation-error` with one 
`errors` entry per parameter; unexpected failures return a generic **500**.
```bash
B=http://localhost:8000/api/v1/products
curl -s "$B?size=5"                                               # first 5 by name
curl -s "$B?page=2&size=5"                                        # next 5
curl -s "$B?search=ring%20binder"                                 # all ring binder variants
curl -s "$B?category=electronics&sort=price&direction=desc"       # most expensive electronics first
curl -s "$B?minPrice=10&maxPrice=50&currency=EUR&sort=price"      # 10.00 to 50.00 EUR, cheapest first
curl -s "$B?search=black&category=ELECTRONICS&maxPrice=100"       # combined filters
curl -s "$B?size=500"                                             # 400: size must be at most 100
```
With `jq` installed, `curl -s "$B?size=100" | jq -r '.items[] | [.sku, .name, .price.amount] | @tsv'` 
prints a compact table.
```powershell
$page = Invoke-RestMethod 'http://localhost:8000/api/v1/products?
category=electronics&sort=price&direction=desc&size=5'
$page.totalItems
$page.items | Format-Table sku, name, @{n='price'; e={$_.price.amount}}
```
In Swagger UI, expand **GET /api/v1/products**, choose **Try it out**, and fill in the parameters; 
`sort` and `direction` are drop-down lists.
## Test
''')

'''The PostgreSQL tests cover Alembic upgrade, downgrade and re-upgrade, committed rows, exact prices,

UUID, pagination, filtering, and sorting. Add read-specific indexes through a new Alembic revision.
''', '''- **Read indexes.** The list queries do not add indexes; the sample catalog is small, and each 
list request counts all matching rows. For large catalogs, add a new Alembic revision with partial 
indexes `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` on the sort keys plus `id`, for example `(name, id)`, and on 
`lower(category)`. Substring search needs a `pg_trgm` GIN index to avoid sequential scans. For deep 
paging, consider keyset (cursor) pagination on the same `(sort key, id)` order.
''')
rep('''- **Soft delete** can use the nullable `deleted_at` column and the existing status consistency 
checks. Retain the SKU reservation and exclude deleted products from future reads.''',
'''- **Soft delete** can use the nullable `deleted_at` column and the existing status consistency 
checks. Retain the SKU reservation. Get and List already exclude rows with `deleted_at` set.''')

ASSISTANT
I added both endpoints, and the full suite passes against PostgreSQL 16: **115 passed, 0 skipped**. I also rebuilt 
everything from scratch (new database cluster and Python environment) using only the README's commands, 
and got the same result.
**Baseline first:** the existing suite gave 27 passed with the 4 PostgreSQL tests skipped. Once I set up a local 
database, all 31 passed.
### What I added
**`GET /api/v1/products/{productId}`**
- Returns a product in exactly the same shape as the create response. Following the `Location` header from a 
POST returns an identical body.
- Soft-deleted products get the same 404 as IDs that never existed (`urn:inventra:problem:product-not-found`).
- An ID that isn't a UUID gets a 400 error in the existing style instead of FastAPI's default 422.
**`GET /api/v1/products`**
- **Paging:** `page` starts at 1; `size` defaults to 20, max 100. The response includes `items`, `page`, `size`, 
`totalItems` and `totalPages`.
- **`search`:** case-insensitive; every word must appear in the SKU, name or description. `ring binder blue` finds 
"A4 Ring Binder - Blue", and `%` and `_` are matched as ordinary characters.
- **Filters:** `category` (exact match, case-insensitive), `minPrice`/`maxPrice` (both inclusive), and `currency`.
- **Sorting:** `sort` and `direction` appear as drop-downs in Swagger UI. Ties are always broken by product ID, so 
paging never repeats or skips a product. This matters here because all 115 seeded products have the same 
creation timestamp. When I removed that tiebreaker as a test, 5 sort tests failed against the real database.
- Soft-deleted products are always excluded, and the count and the page are read from the same database 
snapshot so the totals match the items.
The create endpoint's behaviour is unchanged. I added no migration, so stored data is untouched; restarting 
against the existing database changed nothing.
### Tests and README
- **API tests (72, no database):** response format (exact prices, UTC timestamps, same shape as create), 404 and 
400 errors, 22 kinds of invalid parameter, totals, and the Swagger/OpenAPI document.
- **PostgreSQL tests (43):** create then fetch; soft-deleted products hidden but still stored; gap-free, duplicate-
free paging for every sort field and direction; and search, category, price and currency results checked against 
`data/sample-products.json`.
- I deliberately broke key behaviours one at a time (tiebreaker, soft-delete filter, wildcard handling, inclusive price 
bounds, case-insensitive category), and the tests caught each one.
- **README:** setup for Linux without Docker or root (a user-owned PostgreSQL under `/tmp`), plus curl, 
PowerShell and Swagger examples. Running the examples caught one bug of mine: the snippet that reads the 
`Location` header returned nothing. It's fixed and re-tested.
### Decisions worth checking
- **Existing test updated:** `test_openapi` asserted that POST was the only operation, so it now expects GET too.
- **Wider `status` in the API docs:** the product status now documents `ACTIVE`, `INACTIVE` and 
`DISCONTINUED`, and this also shows on POST's docs. POST still always returns `ACTIVE`. Without this, reading an 
inactive product would have caused a 500 error.
- **Unknown query parameters are rejected** with a 400, matching how create rejects unknown fields. A typo like 
`categroy` won't silently return everything.
- **`currency` filter:** you didn't ask for it, but price bounds compare amounts without currency conversion, so 
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript

the filter makes them meaningful.
- **Defaults:** sorted by name ascending, and a blank `search` or `category` is ignored.
- **No new indexes.** With 115 products they aren't needed; the README lists what to add if the catalog grows 
large.

- **PowerShell examples:** PowerShell isn't installed here, so those are untested.

--- PAGE 90 ---
ASSISTANT
In this sandbox both are already done: the virtual environment has all dependencies installed, and PostgreSQL is 
running on `127.0.0.1:5432`. I just checked both. Here's how to do it yourself.
One thing to know first: **the app doesn't start PostgreSQL for you.** On startup it connects to the database and 
runs migrations, and if it can't connect, it stops with an error. So start PostgreSQL first, then the app.
## 1. Install the dependencies
Run these from the project folder:
**Linux / macOS (this sandbox)**
python3.14 -m venv .venv                             # create a virtual environment (once)
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
**Windows (PowerShell)**
py -3.14 -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
`requirements.txt` is enough to run the app. To also run the tests, install `requirements-dev.txt` instead. It includes 
everything in `requirements.txt` plus `pytest` and `httpx`.
## 2. Start PostgreSQL, then the app
**This sandbox (no sudo, so you run your own database server under `/tmp`)**
Start the database:
export PG_BIN=/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin PGDATA=/tmp/inventra-pg/data
"$PG_BIN/pg_ctl" -D "$PGDATA" -l /tmp/inventra-pg/server.log -w start \
  -o "-c listen_addresses=127.0.0.1 -c port=5432 -c unix_socket_directories=/tmp/inventra-pg"
"$PG_BIN/pg_isready" -h 127.0.0.1 -p 5432           # should say "accepting connections"
Then start the app:
export DATABASE_URL='postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn inventory_management_service.main:app --reload
Then open http://localhost:8000/docs. To stop the database, run `" PGDATA" stop`.
- If `pg_ctl start` says the server is already running, that's fine.
- The database files live under `/tmp`, so they're lost if the sandbox resets. In that case, redo the one-time setup 
P G  IN /pg  tl" −B c D"
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 90/393

--- PAGE 91 ---
in the README section **"Run on Linux without Docker or root"**. It creates the database server, the `inventra` 
user and the databases.
**Windows**
The PostgreSQL installer sets PostgreSQL up as a Windows service that normally starts automatically. To check or 
start it (the service name depends on your version, e.g. `postgresql-x64-18`):
Get-Service postgresql*
Start-Service postgresql-x64-18                 # may need an Administrator terminal
Then start the app:
$env:DATABASE_URL = 'postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m uvicorn inventory_management_service.main:app --reload
If you haven't yet, create the `inventra` user and database once, as shown at the top of the README.
**Linux with root access**
If PostgreSQL is installed as a system package, `sudo systemctl enable --now postgresql` starts it now and on 
every boot.
## If startup fails
A connection error at startup usually means PostgreSQL isn't running, or `DATABASE_URL` isn't set in the current 
terminal. You have to set it again in every new terminal. Run `pg_isready` to check the database.
If you'd like, I can add a small script that starts PostgreSQL when needed, waits until it's ready and then launches 
the app, so it's one command.

the README examples.

"$PG_BIN/pg_isready" -q -h 127.0.0.1 -p 5432 || "$PG_BIN/pg_ctl" -D "$PGDATA" -l /tmp/inventra-
pg/server.log -w start \

--- PAGE 98 ---
ASSISTANT
The errors happen because the **`DATABASE_URL` environment variable isn't set** in the terminal where you start 
the app. With it set, the app starts normally; I tested both cases in the sandbox.
## What happens
When the app starts, it runs the database migrations first. To connect, it reads the database address from 
`DATABASE_URL` (in `inventory_management_service/db.py`). If the variable is missing, Python raises an error, the 
app refuses to start, and you see this:
  File ".../inventory_management_service/db.py", line 14, in engine
    return create_engine(os.environ["DATABASE_URL"], pool_pre_ping=True)
KeyError: 'DATABASE_URL'
ERROR:    Application startup failed. Exiting.
Most of the long traceback above those lines is the framework's internal steps. The last line, `KeyError: 
'DATABASE_URL'`, is the actual cause.
An `export` only lasts for the terminal it was typed in. So the variable is lost whenever you open a new terminal, 
or if you skipped the `export` line before starting the app.
## Fix
Set the variable and start the app **in the same terminal**, from the project folder:
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service
export DATABASE_URL='postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn inventory_management_service.main:app --reload
When I ran this, the log showed `Application startup complete.`, Swagger UI at http://localhost:8000/docs loaded, 
and the product list returned 117 products.
Or set it for one run only:
DATABASE_URL='postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?connect_timeout=5' \
  .venv/bin/python -m uvicorn inventory_management_service.main:app --reload
## If you see a different error
- **`connection refused` / `OperationalError`:** PostgreSQL isn't running. Check with 
`/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin/pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -p 5432` and start it as in the previous step.
- **`address already in use`:** another copy of the app is already running on port 8000. Stop it with Ctrl+C in its 
terminal, or add `--port 8001` to the command. I stopped my test server, so port 8000 is free now.
- **`No module named inventory_management_service`:** you're not in the project folder. `cd` into it first.
If you'd like, I can make this permanent with a small change that reports a clear "DATABASE_URL is not set"

--- PAGE 102 ---
ASSISTANT
The serializer always emits `"id": str(product.id)`, so the current code can't leave it out. The most likely explanation 
is placement. `id` is the **8th** field, after the multi-line `attributes` object, so in Swagger UI's response body it 
sits mid-object and is easy to miss. The other possibility is an older copy of the code on your desktop; I'll give 
you a check for that at the end.
My fix: make `id` the **first** field of every product representation (create, get, list items and the Swagger 
schema), driven by one shared field order so they can't drift apart. Tests will then lock in that `id` is present, first, 
and correct.
Before writing the exhaustive tests, I'll probe the current create and get behaviour at the boundaries, so the tests 
assert real behaviour rather than guesses:

ASSISTANT
The probe is too noisy: my stub raises on purpose, so the 500s mean "passed validation", and the service logs 
each traceback. Also, httpx can't encode a lone surrogate. I'll rerun with logging muted, a stub that returns a 
marker, and the surrogate sent as a raw JSON escape:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
s = open("/tmp/probe2.py").read()
s = s.replace('m.create_product = lambda s, c: (_ for _ in ()).throw(RuntimeError("stub reached"))',
 'import logging; logging.disable(logging.CRITICAL)\nclass Reached(Exception): 
pass\n@m.app.exception_handler(Reached)\nasync def reached(request, error):\n    from 
starlette.responses import JSONResponse\n    return JSONResponse({"type": "VALID (reached service)"}, 
status_code=299)\ndef stub(s, c): raise Reached()\nm.create_product = stub')
s = s.replace('("name surrogate", {"name": "\\ud800"}),', '')
s += '''
show("name lone surrogate (raw)", c.post(P, content=json.dumps(base).replace('"n"', '"\\\\ud800"'), 
headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"}))
show("attr key surrogate (raw)", c.post(P, content=json.dumps(base).replace('{}', '{"\\\\ud800": 
"x"}'), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"}))
'''
open("/tmp/probe2.py", "w").write(s)
EOF

ASSISTANT
The probe gave me the actual behaviour at every boundary. One pre-existing oddity: an attribute key over 64 
characters is rejected correctly, but with Pydantic's misleading message "should have at most 64 *items*". It's 
unrelated to this task, so I'll leave it alone and mention it.
Now the fix. Today POST builds its body one way (re-validating through `ProductResponse`) and GET another 
(`product_data`). I'll make **one serializer produce every product representation** with `id` first, have POST use it 
too, and put `id` first in the OpenAPI schema so Swagger's example matches. Here is the current POST handler:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && sed -n '/^@app.post/,/^default_openapi/p' 
inventory_management_service/main.py

Function Results: bash
@app.post(
    PRODUCTS,
    status_code=201,
    response_model=ProductResponse,
    responses={
        201: {"headers": {"Location": {"schema": {"type": "string", "format": "uri"}}}},
        **problem_responses,
    },
    openapi_extra={
        "requestBody": {
            "required": True,
            "content": {"application/json": {"schema": request_schema}},
        }
    },
    tags=["Products"],
)
def post_product(
    command: Annotated[CreateProduct, Depends(parse_product)],
    session: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_session)],
    request: Request,
):
    product = create_product(session, command)
    result = ProductResponse(
        **command.model_dump(by_alias=True, exclude={"name", "description", "category"}),
        id=product.id,
        name=product.name,
        description=product.description,
        category=product.category,
        status=product.status,
        createdAt=product.created_at,
        updatedAt=product.updated_at,
        version=product.version,
    )
    data = result.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)
    # Keep the decimal exact and emit a JSON number, as in the original API.
    data["price"]["amount"] = exact_amount(product.price_amount)
    base = str(request.base_url).rstrip("/")
    return Response(
        simplejson.dumps(data, use_decimal=True),
        status_code=201,
        media_type="application/json",
        headers={"Location": f"{base}{PRODUCTS}/{product.id}"},
    )
def exact_amount(amount: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    return Decimal(format(amount.normalize(), "f"))
timestamp_json = TypeAdapter(datetime)
def product_data(product: Product) -> dict:
    """Serialize a stored product with the same representation as the create response."""
    return {
        "sku": product.sku,
        "name": product.name,
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 112/393

--- PAGE 113 ---
        "description": product.description,
        "category": product.category,
        "price": {"amount": exact_amount(product.price_amount), "currency": product.price_currency},
        "unitOfMeasure": product.unit_of_measure,
        "attributes": product.attributes,
        "id": str(product.id),
        "status": product.status,
        # The driver returns timestamptz in the session time zone; the API emits UTC.
        "createdAt": timestamp_json.dump_python(product.created_at.astimezone(UTC), mode="json"),
        "updatedAt": timestamp_json.dump_python(product.updated_at.astimezone(UTC), mode="json"),
        "version": product.version,
    }
def json_response(data) -> Response:
    return Response(simplejson.dumps(data, use_decimal=True), media_type="application/json")
def product_query(query: Annotated[ProductQuery, Query()]) -> ProductQuery:
    if query.min_price is not None and query.max_price is not None and query.min_price > 
query.max_price:
        raise RequestValidationError([{
            "type": "value_error",
            "loc": ("query", "minPrice"),
            "msg": "must not be greater than maxPrice",
            "input": str(query.min_price),
        }])
    return query
read_problem_responses = problem_docs({
    400: "Invalid path or query parameter",
    500: "Unexpected server error",
})
@app.get(
    PRODUCTS,
    response_model=ProductPage,
    responses=read_problem_responses,
    tags=["Products"],
    summary="List products",
    description=(
        "Returns one page of products that are not soft-deleted. Filters combine with AND. "
        "Sorting is stable: ties are broken by id, so pages neither overlap nor skip products."
    ),
)
def get_products(
    query: Annotated[ProductQuery, Depends(product_query)],
    session: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_session)],
):
    result = list_products(session, query)
    return json_response({
        "items": [product_data(product) for product in result.items],
        "page": query.page,
        "size": query.size,
        "totalItems": result.total,
        "totalPages": -(-result.total // query.size),
    })

@app.get(
    PRODUCTS + "/{productId}",
    response_model=ProductResponse,
    responses=problem_docs({
        400: "Malformed product ID",
        404: "Product does not exist or is deleted",
        500: "Unexpected server error",
    }, productId={"type": "string", "format": "uuid"}),
    tags=["Products"],
    summary="Get a product by ID",
)
def get_product_by_id(
    product_id: Annotated[UUID, Path(alias="productId", description="Product UUID.")],
    session: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_session)],
):
    return json_response(product_data(get_product(session, product_id)))

I now have the exact behaviour for every case. One framework quirk to note: a 405 on `/api/v1/products` sends 
`Allow: POST` even though GET also exists there. Starlette reports only the first matching route. It's harmless and 
pre-existing, so the tests assert the 405 problem document but not that header's value for the collection.
Next, the shared test double. The service layer needs a Session, so I'll add an in-memory `FakeSession` to 
`conftest.py`. It records calls and emulates the initial-version assignment via the real mapper's 
`version_id_generator`, so it can't drift from the model configuration:

''', '''from sqlalchemy.engine import make_url
from inventory_management_service.models import Product
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
class FakeSession:
    """In-memory stand-in for the SQLAlchemy Session used by the service layer.
    It supports the calls the services make (begin, add, connection, scalar, and
    scalars), records them in ``events`` in call order, and returns queued
    ``results``. This lets unit tests check service logic and the statements it
    issues without PostgreSQL.
    """
    def __init__(self, *results):
        self.results = list(results)
        self.events = []
        self.added = []
        self.commit_error = None
        self.in_transaction = False
    @contextmanager
    def begin(self):
        assert not self.in_transaction, "nested transactions are not expected"
        self.in_transaction = True
        self.events.append(("begin",))
        try:
            yield self
            if self.commit_error is not None:
                raise self.commit_error
            # A real flush assigns the initial optimistic-locking version.
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 122/393

--- PAGE 123 ---
            for instance in self.added:
                if instance.version is None:
                    instance.version = Product.__mapper__.version_id_generator(None)
            self.events.append(("commit",))
        finally:
            self.in_transaction = False
    def add(self, instance):
        assert self.in_transaction, "add() must run inside a transaction"
        self.added.append(instance)
        self.events.append(("add", instance))
    def connection(self, execution_options=None):
        assert self.in_transaction, "connection() must run inside a transaction"
        self.events.append(("connection", execution_options or {}))
    def scalar(self, statement):
        assert self.in_transaction, "queries must run inside a transaction"
        self.events.append(("scalar", statement))
        return self.results.pop(0)
    def scalars(self, statement):
        assert self.in_transaction, "queries must run inside a transaction"
        self.events.append(("scalars", statement))
        return iter(self.results.pop(0))
    @property
    def statements(self):
        return [event[1] for event in self.events if event[0] in ("scalar", "scalars")]
@pytest.fixture
def fake_session():
    return FakeSession()
''')
p.write_text(s)

def stored_product(**changes) -> Product:
    """A fully populated product, as the services return it."""
    created = datetime(2025, 3, 1, 12, 0, 0, 123456, tzinfo=UTC)
    values = dict(
        id=uuid7(), sku="LAP-DELL-1", name="Dell XPS", description=None, category="ELECTRONICS",
        price_amount=Decimal("1499.9900"), price_currency="EUR", unit_of_measure="PIECE",
        attributes={"brand": "Dell"}, status="ACTIVE", created_at=created, updated_at=created,
        deleted_at=None, version=0,
    )
    return Product(**values | changes)
class ServiceStub:
    """Stand-in for a service function. Records each call, then returns or raises.
    ``result`` is either the value to return or a function of the call argument.
    """
    def __init__(self, result):
        self.result = result
        self.error = None
        self.calls = []
    def __call__(self, session, argument):
        self.calls.append(argument)
        if self.error is not None:
            raise self.error
        return self.result(argument) if callable(self.result) else self.result
@pytest.fixture
def services(monkeypatch):
    """Stub the three product services with realistic default results."""
    stubs = SimpleNamespace(
        create=ServiceStub(lambda command: stored_product(sku=command.sku)),
        get=ServiceStub(lambda product_id: stored_product(id=product_id)),
        list=ServiceStub(ProductSlice([], 0)),
    )
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.create_product", stubs.create)
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.get_product", stubs.get)
    monkeypatch.setattr("inventory_management_service.main.list_products", stubs.list)
    return stubs
def post_raw(client, raw, content_type="application/json"):
    """POST an exact request body, bypassing client-side JSON encoding."""
    headers = {"Content-Type": content_type} if content_type is not None else {}
    return client.post(PRODUCTS, content=raw, headers=headers)
def assert_problem(response, status, kind=None):
    """Assert an RFC 9457 problem document and return its body."""
    assert response.status_code == status, response.text
    assert response.headers["content-type"] == PROBLEM_MEDIA
    body = response.json()
    assert body["type"] == (f"urn:inventra:problem:{kind}" if kind else "about:blank")
    assert body["status"] == status
    assert {"title", "detail", "instance"} <= set(body)
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 125/393

--- PAGE 126 ---
    return body
def error_fields(body):
    return [item["field"] for item in body.get("errors", [])]

def test_create_returns_created_product_and_location(client, payload, services):
    product = stored_product()
    services.create.result = product
    response = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    assert response.headers["content-type"] == "application/json"
    assert response.headers["location"] == f"http://testserver{PRODUCTS}/{product.id}"
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    assert list(data) == PRODUCT_FIELDS
    assert data == {
        "id": str(product.id),
        "sku": "LAP-DELL-1",
        "name": "Dell XPS",
        "description": None,
        "category": "ELECTRONICS",
        "price": {"amount": Decimal("1499.99"), "currency": "EUR"},
        "unitOfMeasure": "PIECE",
        "attributes": {"brand": "Dell"},
        "status": "ACTIVE",
        "createdAt": "2025-03-01T12:00:00.123456Z",
        "updatedAt": "2025-03-01T12:00:00.123456Z",
        "version": 0,
    }
def test_create_passes_canonical_command_to_service(client, payload, services):
    # A raw JSON number keeps all 19 digits; it is never parsed as a binary float.
    raw = json.dumps(payload).replace('"999999999999999.9999"', "999999999999999.9999")
    assert post_raw(client, raw).status_code == 201
    (command,) = services.create.calls
    assert command.sku == "LAP-DELL-1"
    assert command.price.amount == Decimal("999999999999999.9999")
    assert command.price.currency == "EUR"
    assert command.unit_of_measure is Unit.PIECE
    assert command.attributes == {"brand": "Dell"}
def test_create_normalizes_input_end_to_end(client, payload, fake_session):
    """Run the real create service on a fake session: request to response, no stubs."""
    app.dependency_overrides[get_session] = lambda: fake_session
    response = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    (product,) = fake_session.added
    assert data["id"] == str(product.id)
    assert UUID(data["id"]).version == 7
    assert response.headers["location"].endswith(f"{PRODUCTS}/{data['id']}")
    assert data["sku"] == "LAP-DELL-1"
    assert data["name"] == "Dell XPS"
    assert data["description"] is None

def test_create_duplicate_sku(client, payload, services):
    services.create.error = DuplicateSku("LAP-DELL-1")
    response = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert assert_problem(response, 409, "duplicate-sku") == {
        "type": "urn:inventra:problem:duplicate-sku",
        "title": "Duplicate SKU",
        "status": 409,
        "detail": "A product with SKU 'LAP-DELL-1' already exists.",
        "instance": PRODUCTS,

    }

def test_create_unexpected_failure_is_generic_and_logged(client, payload, services, caplog):
    services.create.error = RuntimeError("connection to 10.0.0.5 failed: password=secret")
    with caplog.at_level(logging.ERROR, logger="inventory_management_service.main"):
        response = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert assert_problem(response, 500) == {
        "type": "about:blank",
        "title": "Internal Server Error",
        "status": 500,
        "detail": "An unexpected error occurred.",
        "instance": PRODUCTS,
    }
    assert "secret" not in response.text
    # The details go to the log instead of the client.
    (record,) = [record for record in caplog.records if "Request failed" in record.getMessage()]
    assert record.getMessage() == f"Request failed: POST {PRODUCTS}"
    assert "password=secret" in str(record.exc_info[1])

def test_get_product_contract(client, services):
    # The driver returns timestamptz in the session time zone; the API must emit UTC.
    local = timezone(timedelta(hours=2))
    product = stored_product(
        price_amount=Decimal("999999999999999.9999"),
        created_at=datetime(2025, 3, 1, 14, 0, tzinfo=local),
        updated_at=datetime(2025, 3, 2, 14, 0, 0, 5, tzinfo=local),
        version=3,
    )
    services.get.result = product
    response = client.get(f"{PRODUCTS}/{product.id}")
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    assert response.headers["content-type"] == "application/json"
    assert services.get.calls == [product.id]
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    assert list(data) == PRODUCT_FIELDS
    assert data == {
        "id": str(product.id),
        "sku": "LAP-DELL-1",
        "name": "Dell XPS",
        "description": None,
        "category": "ELECTRONICS",
        "price": {"amount": Decimal("999999999999999.9999"), "currency": "EUR"},
        "unitOfMeasure": "PIECE",
        "attributes": {"brand": "Dell"},
        "status": "ACTIVE",
        "createdAt": "2025-03-01T12:00:00Z",
        "updatedAt": "2025-03-02T12:00:00.000005Z",
        "version": 3,
    }
