def test_list_defaults_and_envelope(client, services):
    items = [stored_product(sku="A-1"), stored_product(sku="B-2", price_amount=Decimal("0.5000"))]
    services.list.result = ProductSlice(items, 41)
    response = client.get(PRODUCTS)
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    assert response.headers["content-type"] == "application/json"
    (query,) = services.list.calls
    assert (query.page, query.size, query.sort, query.direction) == (1, 20, ProductSort.NAME, 
SortDirection.ASC)
    assert (query.search, query.category, query.min_price, query.max_price, query.currency) == (None,) 
* 5
    data = response.json(parse_float=Decimal)
    assert list(data) == ["items", "page", "size", "totalItems", "totalPages"]
    assert (data["page"], data["size"], data["totalItems"], data["totalPages"]) == (1, 20, 41, 3)
    assert [item["sku"] for item in data["items"]] == ["A-1", "B-2"]
    assert [item["id"] for item in data["items"]] == [str(item.id) for item in items]
    assert data["items"][1]["price"]["amount"] == Decimal("0.5")
@pytest.mark.parametrize(("total", "size", "pages"), [(0, 20, 0), (1, 20, 1), (20, 20, 1), (21, 20, 
2), (115, 7, 17)])

    services.list.result = ProductSlice([], total)
    response = client.get(PRODUCTS, params={"size": size, "page": 99})
    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert response.json() == {"items": [], "page": 99, "size": size, "totalItems": total, 
"totalPages": pages}

def test_list_rejects_invalid_parameters(client, services, params, field):
    body = assert_problem(client.get(PRODUCTS, params=params), 400, "validation-error")
    assert body["instance"] == PRODUCTS
    assert error_fields(body) == [field]
    assert services.list.calls == []
def test_list_reports_every_invalid_parameter(client, services):
    body = assert_problem(client.get(PRODUCTS, params={"page": "0", "size": "500", "sort": "weight"}), 
400, "validation-error")
    assert error_fields(body) == ["page", "size", "sort"]
    assert body["detail"] == "The request contains 3 invalid parameter(s)."
def test_list_unexpected_failure_is_generic(client, services):
    services.list.error = RuntimeError("connection lost: secret details")
    response = client.get(PRODUCTS)
    assert assert_problem(response, 500)["detail"] == "An unexpected error occurred."
    assert "secret" not in response.text

def represent(client, services, payload, endpoint, product):
    """Return (raw body, product JSON) as produced by one endpoint for `product`."""

@pytest.mark.parametrize("endpoint", ENDPOINTS)
@pytest.mark.parametrize(("stored", "emitted"), [
    ("249.0000", "249"), ("0.0000", "0"), ("24.9000", "24.9"), ("0.0001", "0.0001"),

def test_amount_is_an_exact_json_number(client, services, payload, endpoint, stored, emitted):
    text, data = represent(client, services, payload, endpoint,

    assert f'"amount": {emitted},' in text          # a JSON number, not a string
    assert data["price"]["amount"] == Decimal(emitted)
@pytest.mark.parametrize("endpoint", ENDPOINTS)
def test_timestamps_are_utc(client, services, payload, endpoint):
    local = timezone(timedelta(hours=-5))
    product = stored_product(
        created_at=datetime(2025, 1, 1, 19, 30, tzinfo=local),
        updated_at=datetime(2025, 1, 2, 8, 0, 0, 250000, tzinfo=local),
    )
    _, data = represent(client, services, payload, endpoint, product)
    assert data["createdAt"] == "2025-01-02T00:30:00Z"
    assert data["updatedAt"] == "2025-01-02T13:00:00.250000Z"

def test_openapi_read_operations(client):
    schema = client.get("/openapi.json").json()
    listing = schema["paths"][PRODUCTS]["get"]
    assert set(listing["responses"]) == {"200", "400", "500"}
    assert [parameter["name"] for parameter in listing["parameters"]] == [
        "page", "size", "search", "category", "minPrice", "maxPrice", "currency", "sort", "direction",
    ]
    assert listing["responses"]["200"]["content"]["application/json"]["schema"] == {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/ProductPage"
    }
    single = schema["paths"][f"{PRODUCTS}/{{productId}}"]
    assert set(single) == {"get"}
    assert set(single["get"]["responses"]) == {"200", "400", "404", "500"}
    assert single["get"]["responses"]["200"]["content"]["application/json"]["schema"] == {
        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/ProductResponse"
    }
    assert "application/problem+json" in single["get"]["responses"]["404"]["content"]
    components = schema["components"]["schemas"]
    assert "HTTPValidationError" not in components
    assert components["ProductResponse"]["properties"]["price"] == {"$ref": 
"#/components/schemas/PriceResponse"}
    assert set(components["ProductPage"]["required"]) == {"items", "page", "size", "totalItems", 
"totalPages"}

"""Unit tests for the service layer. No database is used.
The services run against FakeSession (see conftest.py), which records every call.
SQL statements are compiled with the PostgreSQL dialect, so the tests check the SQL
that would be sent: filters, ordering, paging, and parameters. test_postgres.py
checks the same behaviour against a real database.
"""
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from decimal import Decimal
from types import SimpleNamespace
from uuid import uuid7
import pytest
from sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql
from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError, OperationalError
from inventory_management_service.models import Product
from inventory_management_service.schemas import CreateProduct, ProductQuery
from inventory_management_service.service import (
    DuplicateSku,
    ProductNotFound,
    ProductSlice,
    create_product,
    get_product,
    list_products,
)
SORT_COLUMNS = {
    "name": "name", "sku": "sku", "category": "category", "price": "price_amount",
    "createdAt": "created_at", "updatedAt": "updated_at",
}
def compiled(statement):
    """Return the PostgreSQL SQL text (whitespace-normalized) and bound parameters."""
    result = statement.compile(dialect=postgresql.dialect())
    return " ".join(str(result).split()), result.params
def query(**params) -> ProductQuery:
    return ProductQuery.model_validate(params)
def integrity_error(sqlstate, constraint):
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 138/393

--- PAGE 139 ---
    """An IntegrityError whose driver error looks like psycopg's."""
    original = SimpleNamespace(sqlstate=sqlstate, diag=SimpleNamespace(constraint_name=constraint))
    return IntegrityError("INSERT INTO products ...", {}, original)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# create_product
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def test_create_maps_the_command_to_a_new_product(fake_session, payload):
    command = CreateProduct.model_validate(payload)
    before = datetime.now(UTC)
    product = create_product(fake_session, command)
    assert fake_session.added == [product]
    assert [event[0] for event in fake_session.events] == ["begin", "add", "commit"]
    assert product.id.version == 7
    assert product.sku == "LAP-DELL-1"
    assert product.name == "Dell XPS"                 # trimmed
    assert product.description is None                # blank becomes null
    assert product.category == "ELECTRONICS"          # trimmed
    assert product.price_amount == Decimal("999999999999999.9999")
    assert product.price_currency == "EUR"
    assert product.unit_of_measure == "PIECE"         # stored as the plain enum value
    assert type(product.unit_of_measure) is str
    assert product.attributes == {"brand": "Dell"}
    assert product.status == "ACTIVE"
    assert product.deleted_at is None
    assert product.version == 0
    assert product.created_at == product.updated_at
    assert product.created_at.tzinfo is UTC
    assert before <= product.created_at <= datetime.now(UTC)
def test_create_trims_the_description(fake_session, payload):
    command = CreateProduct.model_validate(payload | {"description": "  15-inch laptop  "})
    assert create_product(fake_session, command).description == "15-inch laptop"
def test_create_keeps_a_missing_description_null(fake_session, payload):
    del payload["description"]
    assert create_product(fake_session, CreateProduct.model_validate(payload)).description is None
def test_create_assigns_distinct_time_ordered_ids(payload):
    from conftest import FakeSession
    command = CreateProduct.model_validate(payload)
    ids = [create_product(FakeSession(), command).id for _ in range(50)]
    assert len(set(ids)) == 50
    assert ids == sorted(ids)                          # UUIDv7 sorts by creation time
def test_create_translates_a_duplicate_sku(fake_session, payload):
    fake_session.commit_error = integrity_error("23505", "uk_products_sku")
    with pytest.raises(DuplicateSku) as raised:
        create_product(fake_session, CreateProduct.model_validate(payload))
    assert raised.value.sku == "LAP-DELL-1"           # the canonical SKU
    assert isinstance(raised.value.__cause__, IntegrityError)

@pytest.mark.parametrize(("sqlstate", "constraint"), [
    ("23505", "pk_products"),                          # unique violation of another constraint
    ("23514", "ck_products_name_not_blank"),           # check violation
    ("23502", None),                                   # not-null violation
    (None, None),                                      # driver error without a SQLSTATE
])
def test_create_reraises_other_integrity_errors(fake_session, payload, sqlstate, constraint):
    fake_session.commit_error = integrity_error(sqlstate, constraint)
    with pytest.raises(IntegrityError):
        create_product(fake_session, CreateProduct.model_validate(payload))
def test_create_propagates_database_failures(fake_session, payload):
    fake_session.commit_error = OperationalError("INSERT", {}, Exception("server closed the 
connection"))
    with pytest.raises(OperationalError):
        create_product(fake_session, CreateProduct.model_validate(payload))

def test_get_returns_the_product(fake_session):
    product = Product(id=uuid7())
    fake_session.results.append(product)
    assert get_product(fake_session, product.id) is product
    assert [event[0] for event in fake_session.events] == ["begin", "scalar", "commit"]
def test_get_selects_one_non_deleted_product_by_id(fake_session):
    product_id = uuid7()
    fake_session.results.append(Product(id=product_id))
    get_product(fake_session, product_id)
    (statement,) = fake_session.statements
    sql, params = compiled(statement)
    assert sql.startswith("SELECT products.id, products.sku,")
    assert sql.endswith("FROM products WHERE products.id = %(id_1)s::UUID AND products.deleted_at IS 
NULL")
    assert params == {"id_1": product_id}
def test_get_raises_not_found_for_missing_or_deleted_products(fake_session):
    # The query excludes deleted rows, so both cases arrive as "no row".
    product_id = uuid7()
    fake_session.results.append(None)
    with pytest.raises(ProductNotFound) as raised:
        get_product(fake_session, product_id)
    assert raised.value.product_id == product_id

def run_list(fake_session, total, rows=(), **params):
    fake_session.results += [total, list(rows)]
    return list_products(fake_session, query(**params))

def test_list_default_query(fake_session):
    run_list(fake_session, total=30)
    count, page = (compiled(statement) for statement in fake_session.statements)
    assert count == (
        "SELECT count(*) AS count_1 FROM products WHERE products.deleted_at IS NULL", {},
    )
    sql, params = page
    assert sql.endswith(
        "FROM products WHERE products.deleted_at IS NULL "
        "ORDER BY products.name ASC, products.id ASC "
        "LIMIT %(param_1)s OFFSET %(param_2)s"
    )
    assert params == {"param_1": 20, "param_2": 0}
@pytest.mark.parametrize("direction", ["asc", "desc"])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("sort", list(SORT_COLUMNS))
def test_list_orders_by_sort_key_then_id(fake_session, sort, direction):
    run_list(fake_session, total=1, sort=sort, direction=direction)
    sql, _ = compiled(fake_session.statements[1])
    keyword = direction.upper()
    # The unique id tiebreaker, in the same direction, makes the order total.
    assert f"ORDER BY products.{SORT_COLUMNS[sort]} {keyword}, products.id {keyword} LIMIT" in sql

900)])
def test_list_pages_with_offset_and_limit(fake_session, page, size, offset):
    run_list(fake_session, total=10_000, page=page, size=size)
    _, params = compiled(fake_session.statements[1])
    assert params == {"param_1": size, "param_2": offset}

def test_list_requires_every_search_term(fake_session):
    run_list(fake_session, total=0, search="  ring   binder\tblue ")
    sql, params = compiled(fake_session.statements[0])
    # One OR group per term, combined with AND.
    assert sql.count("products.sku ILIKE") == sql.count("products.name ILIKE") == 3
    assert sql.count(") AND (") == 2
    assert sorted(value for key, value in params.items() if key.startswith("sku_")) == ["binder", 
"blue", "ring"]

    created = client.post(PRODUCTS, json=payload)
    assert created.status_code == 201, created.text
    fetched = client.get(created.headers["location"])
    assert fetched.status_code == 200, fetched.text
    # Stored values, including the 19-digit price and microsecond timestamps, round-trip
    # exactly, and both responses use the same field order.
    assert fetched.text == created.text
    data = fetched.json()
    assert list(data)[0] == "id"
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        stored_id = connection.scalar(text("SELECT id FROM products WHERE sku = 'LAP-DELL-1'"))

--- PAGE 153 ---
ASSISTANT
I couldn't reproduce a missing `id`: in this sandbox every GET response already contained it. So I've made it 
impossible to miss, and the tests now guarantee it's always there. Its value was always correct, but it was the 8th 
field, after the `attributes` object, so in Swagger UI it sat in the middle of the response where it's easy to overlook. 
It's now the first field in every response, and all 316 tests pass against PostgreSQL.
**If it's still missing on your desktop**, your copy of the code is probably out of date. Open 
`inventory_management_service/main.py` and find `def product_data`. The first line of the dictionary it returns 
should be `"id": str(product.id),`. If it isn't, copy over the latest `main.py`.
## The fix
- **`id` first in every response.** Create, get by ID and the list items now all use the same code to build a 
product's JSON, so they can't drift apart. Today POST builds its response separately; I checked four tricky inputs 
against the real database, and it returns exactly the same values as before, just with `id` first.
- **Swagger UI matches.** Its example response and schema also show `id` first, marked as required.
## The tests
I kept the existing structure and added one new file:
| File | Tests | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/test_api.py` (rewritten in sections) | 228 | The create, get and list endpoints over HTTP, without a database 
|
| `tests/test_service.py` (new) | 43 | The business logic and the SQL it generates, using a fake in-memory database 
session (added to `conftest.py`) |
| `tests/test_postgres.py` | 43 | The full stack against real PostgreSQL; the create-then-get check now requires 
identical responses, `id` first included |
| `tests/test_startup.py` | 2 | Unchanged |
- **Create:** successful creation and the `Location` header, every validation rule at its exact limit (e.g. a 64-
character SKU passes, 65 fails), every unit of measure, missing fields, wrong types, unknown fields, broken JSON, 
unsupported content types, and the 409, 500 and 405 errors. Check that internal error details don't leak to the 
client.
- **Get by ID:** the full response, the UUID formats accepted, every non-deleted status, malformed IDs, and not-
found.
- **List:** default values, every sort option, the filters, invalid parameters, and page counting.
- **All three endpoints:** `id` is present and first, the product looks identical in each, prices are exact numbers, 
and times are in UTC.
I also deliberately introduced 18 realistic bugs one at a time, such as dropping `id`, untrimmed names, or 
returning deleted products. The tests caught every one, and moving `id` back to 8th place breaks 5 of them.
## Two small existing issues I left alone
- A 405 ("method not allowed") response on `/api/v1/products` lists only `POST` as allowed, although GET works 
too.
- An attribute key over 64 characters is rejected correctly, but the error message says "at most 64 items" instead 
of characters.
Both are cosmetic and outside this change; I can fix either if you want.

ASSISTANT
The 43 skipped tests are the PostgreSQL integration tests, and they skipped because 
**`INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` isn't set where PyCharm runs the tests**. Nothing is broken: the tests skip on 
purpose when no test database is configured. The other 273 tests ran.
## How the report shows this
Your file is a coverage report, which lists the lines that ran rather than why tests were skipped. Its line numbers 
match `tests/conftest.py` exactly:
| Line | Code | Hits |
|---|---|---|
| 94 | `url = os.environ.get("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL")` | 1 |
| 95 | `if not url:` | 1 |
| 96 | `pytest.skip("Set INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL to run PostgreSQL integration tests")` | 1 |
| 97 onwards | reading the URL, connecting to the database, running migrations | **0** |
The skip line ran and nothing after it did. The rest of the report agrees: in `test_postgres.py` only the top-level 
definitions ran, none of the test bodies. The report also shows the run used PyCharm's test runner 
(`_jb_pytest_runner.py`).
I reproduced it in the sandbox. With the variable unset I get your result exactly (**273 passed, 43 skipped**, all 
with that message); with it set, **316 passed**.
## Why it happens in PyCharm
PyCharm's test runner doesn't see variables you set in a terminal such as `$env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL 
= ...`. Each run configuration has its own list of environment variables, and that's where it needs to go.
## Fix
**1. Create the test database once**, if you haven't yet. Run this as the PostgreSQL admin user:
psql -h localhost -U postgres -c "CREATE DATABASE inventra_test"
psql -h localhost -U postgres -d inventra_test -f tests/init-test-db.sql
That second step creates the `inventra_test` login role. The tests insist on a dedicated database and role with that 
name so they can never touch your real data.
**2. Give PyCharm the variable:**
- Go to **Run → Edit Configurations… → Edit configuration templates… → Python tests → pytest**.
- Under **Environment variables**, add:
  
`INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_tes
t`
- Delete your existing pytest run configurations so PyCharm recreates them from the template. The template only 
applies to new configurations; alternatively, add the variable to the one you already use.
**Or run from a PowerShell terminal** in the project folder:
$env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL = 
'postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_test'

ASSISTANT
The settings folder has no saved test run configurations, so it can't tell me what PyCharm passes to the tests. It 
does show PyCharm connecting to PostgreSQL at `localhost:5432/inventra`, so your database server is in the 
expected place.
Environment variables in PyCharm run configurations are easy to get wrong, so I'll remove that dependency. The 
test setup will fall back to a `.env` file in the project root when the variable isn't set. That file is already git-
ignored, and it works the same from PyCharm, PowerShell or anywhere else. Since you're on Windows, the reader 
also handles Windows line endings and the invisible marker Notepad sometimes adds to the start of a file.
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
p = "tests/conftest.py"; s = open(p).read()
def rep(old, new):
    global s
    assert s.count(old) == 1, old
    s = s.replace(old, new)
rep('''ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
''', '''ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
def setting(name):
    """Read a test setting from the environment, falling back to the project's .env file.
    IDE test runners such as PyCharm do not inherit variables set in a terminal, so a
    git-ignored .env file in the project root (lines like NAME=value) also works.
    The environment wins when both are set.
    """
    if os.environ.get(name):
        return os.environ[name]
    path = ROOT / ".env"
    if not path.is_file():
        return None
    # utf-8-sig tolerates the byte-order mark that some Windows editors write.
    for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig").splitlines():
        key, separator, value = line.strip().partition("=")
        if separator and key.strip() == name:
            return value.strip().strip("'\\"") or None
    return None
''')
rep('''    url = os.environ.get("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL")
    if not url:
        pytest.skip("Set INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL to run PostgreSQL integration tests")
''', '''    url = setting("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL")
    if not url:
        pytest.skip(
            "Set INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL, as an environment variable or in the project's "
            ".env file, to run PostgreSQL integration tests"
        )
''')
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF
cat .gitignore; git check-ignore -v .env

ASSISTANT
This is the same cause as before: **`INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` isn't reaching PyCharm's test runner**, so 
the PostgreSQL tests, such as `test_price_filters_are_inclusive[10-50]`, skip themselves. It's a deliberate skip, not an 
error. Setting the variable in a terminal doesn't carry over to tests started from PyCharm, which is easy to trip on, 
so I changed the test setup to avoid relying on it.
## What I changed
- **`tests/conftest.py`:** if the variable isn't set, the tests now also read it from a `.env` file in the project root. If 
both exist, the environment variable wins.
- **Skip message:** it now mentions the `.env` option.
- **`README.md`:** documents the option.
The `.env` file is already git-ignored, so it stays on your machine. In the sandbox, with only a `.env` file saved the 
way Windows Notepad saves it, all **316 tests pass**. A real environment variable still takes priority, and with 
neither set the tests skip as before.
## What to do on your desktop
**1. Copy the updated `tests/conftest.py`** (and `README.md`) into your project.
**2. Create the `.env` file** in the project root, the folder containing `requirements.txt`. From PowerShell in that 
folder:
Set-Content -Path .env -Encoding utf8 -Value 
'INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_te
st'
Use `Set-Content` as shown, not `>` (e.g. `echo ... > .env`). In Windows PowerShell 5.1, `>` writes the file in an 
encoding the tests can't read. You can also create the file in PyCharm (right-click the project folder → New → File 
→ `.env`) and paste that line in.
**3. Create the test database**, if you haven't already. This is a one-time step, run as the PostgreSQL admin user:
psql -h localhost -U postgres -c "CREATE DATABASE inventra_test"
psql -h localhost -U postgres -d inventra_test -f tests/init-test-db.sql
**4. Run the tests again in PyCharm.** No run-configuration changes are needed.
## If you'd rather not use a file
Set the variable in PyCharm itself:
- Go to **Run → Edit Configurations…** and select your pytest configuration.
- Under **Environment variables**, add the name `INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` with the URL above as its 
value.
Existing configurations don't pick up template changes, so edit the one you actually run.
## What to expect
- **All pass:** everything is set up.

--- PAGE 221 ---
ASSISTANT
Two facts are now established from the code:
1. **All three handlers return a finished `Response` object**, so FastAPI's `response_model` never re-serializes or 
filters the body. What the client receives is exactly what `product_data()` builds, and `product_data()` has 
contained `"id": str(product.id)` in every version that had GET routes. The original baseline had no GET routes at 
all.
2. **uvicorn always sets `SO_REUSEADDR`** on its listening socket. On Windows, that option lets a second 
process bind a port another process is already listening on. So a stale server running older or different code can 
coexist with a new one on port 8000. I can't test Windows here, so this is a hypothesis to check on your machine, 
not a finding.
Both tell me the same thing: as written, this code can't produce a GET body without `id`. The cause must be 
outside the serializer: a different process answering, local code that differs from the repository, or something 
about how the response was observed. I'll come back to that with a diagnostic script after the test review.
For the review, I want to replace text-based SQL assertions with structural ones where a DB-free check is still 
worthwhile. I'll check what SQLAlchemy's public `ClauseElement.compare()` actually compares, in particular 
whether it sees bound values and whether it ignores formatting:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python - <<'EOF'
from uuid import uuid7
from sqlalchemy import select, func
from inventory_management_service.models import Product
a, b = uuid7(), uuid7()
stmt = select(Product).where(Product.id == a, Product.deleted_at.is_(None))
w = stmt.whereclause
print("type:", type(w).__name__, "clauses:", len(w.clauses))
print("deleted criterion found:", any(c.compare(Product.deleted_at.is_(None)) for c in w.clauses))
print("id==a matches:", any(c.compare(Product.id == a) for c in w.clauses), "| id==b matches:", 
any(c.compare(Product.id == b) for c in w.clauses))
print("is_not(None) wrongly matches:", any(c.compare(Product.deleted_at.is_not(None)) for c in 
w.clauses))
one = select(func.count()).select_from(Product).where(Product.deleted_at.is_(None))
print("single-criterion whereclause type:", type(one.whereclause).__name__)
print("order_by public?", [n for n in dir(stmt) if "order" in n.lower()])
EOF

--- PAGE 222 ---
ASSISTANT
`compare()` is structural and checks bound values: it matches `id == a`, rejects `id == b`, and distinguishes `IS 
NULL` from `IS NOT NULL`, with no dependence on SQL text. `ORDER BY` has no public accessor, so an ordering 
check needs either PostgreSQL or a narrowly parsed `ORDER BY` clause.
Here's the analysis, before I change anything.
**Assertions that protect real requirements (keeping all of these):**
- **Validation:** every parametrized validation, malformed-JSON, missing-field and media-type case. Also the 
sorted error list with its count in `detail`, and `services.*.calls == []` on rejection (invalid input never reaches 
persistence).
- **Problem documents:** exact-dict checks for 404/409/500 and `Location` on create.
- **Exact prices:** value equality with `Decimal`; the amount is a JSON *number*, not a string; and `249.0000` is 
written as `249`, matching the original create behaviour.
- **PostgreSQL:** filter results compared with expectations computed from `sample-products.json`; paging for 
every sort and direction compared with independent SQL on data with real ties; soft deletion; UTC output; seed 
preservation; migrations; the concurrent-insert race.
- **Database isolation:** the per-test schema and the role-safety checks in the `database` fixture. I'm not 
touching it.
- **Service:** create mapping, duplicate-SKU translation and re-raise, not-found, and REPEATABLE READ 
requested before the first query with both queries in one transaction.
**Brittle assertions:**
- **JSON member order (JSON objects are unordered):** `list(data) == PRODUCT_FIELDS` (3 places), the envelope 
key order, `fetched.text == created.text` (API and PostgreSQL), `list(data)[0] == "id"`, the OpenAPI 
property/`required`/parameter order, and Pydantic's auto-generated `"title": "Id"`. The `'"amount": 249,'` substring 
checks also depend on whitespace and on which key follows the amount.
- **SQL formatting:** exact `SELECT`/`WHERE`/`ORDER BY … LIMIT` strings, bind-parameter names (`%
(id_1)s::UUID`, `param_1`), counting `ILIKE` and `") AND ("` substrings, and SQLAlchemy's choice of `/` as the `LIKE` 
escape character.
- **Internal call sequences:** exact event lists (`begin, add, commit` and so on), `len(statements) == 1` past the 
last page (an optimization, not a requirement), `type(unit_of_measure) is str`, and the exact log-message text.
**Redundant:**
- `test_get_product_matches_create_representation` duplicates 
`test_every_endpoint_returns_the_same_representation`, plus the PostgreSQL `Location` round trip.
- `test_search_finds_exact_variant` duplicates `test_search["ring binder blue"]`, whose computed expectation is 
that same single product.
Each removed SQL check is covered by PostgreSQL tests against independent expectations. I'll keep DB-free 
structural guards for the highest-risk rules: soft-delete filtering, count and page using identical criteria, and the 
`id` tiebreaker. Starting with `test_api.py`:

    global s
    assert s.count(old) == count, (s.count(old), old)
    s = s.replace(old, new)

def criteria(statement):

--- PAGE 228 ---
    """The AND-ed WHERE criteria of a statement, in no particular order."""
    where = statement.whereclause
    return list(where.clauses) if isinstance(where, BooleanClauseList) else [where]
def has_criterion(statement, expected):
    # compare() is structural and includes bound values, so formatting is irrelevant.
    return any(criterion.compare(expected) for criterion in criteria(statement))
def order_keys(statement):
    """ORDER BY as [(column, direction)]. Select has no public accessor for it."""
    sql = str(statement.compile(dialect=postgresql.dialect()))
    clause = re.search(r"ORDER BY (.*?)(?: LIMIT| OFFSET|$)", sql, re.S).group(1)
    return [tuple(key.split()) for key in clause.split(",")]
''')

def test_running_server_serves_ids_and_this_codes_openapi(database):
    """Start the application the way users do (uvicorn in its own process) and call it
    over HTTP. Startup migrations run against the test schema, which is already at head."""
    engine, _ = database
    with engine.connect() as connection:
        schema = connection.scalar(text("SELECT current_schema()"))

## Part 2: diagnosing the missing `id`
I can't see your desktop, so I'll build evidence collection rather than guess. The script below is **read-only**: it 
sends only GET requests, opens a read-only database transaction, and never starts the app's migrations. It 
compares four things:
1. The local code: which files Python imports, their hashes, and whether the serializer emits `id`.
2. The OpenAPI schema the local code would produce.
3. The running server's `/openapi.json` and its actual GET responses.
4. What the local serializer returns for the same product read from your database.
It also lists every process listening on the port, because of the Windows `SO_REUSEADDR` possibility.
Tool Use: str_replace_based_edit_tool
command
create
path
/tmp/diagnose_id.py
file_text
"""Read-only diagnosis for "id is missing from GET responses".
Run it from the project root with the interpreter and DATABASE_URL you use for the server:
    .venv\\Scripts\\python.exe diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]      (Windows)
    .venv/bin/python diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]                (Linux/macOS)
It only sends GET requests and reads the database in a read-only transaction.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import os
import platform
import subprocess
import sys
from decimal import Decimal
from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError
from urllib.parse import urlparse
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd())
BASE = (sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "http://localhost:8000").rstrip("/")
PRODUCTS = "/api/v1/products"
findings = []
def fetch(path):
    request = Request(BASE + path, headers={"Accept": "application/json"})
    try:
        with urlopen(request, timeout=10) as response:
            return response.status, response.headers, response.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except HTTPError as error:

        return error.code, error.headers, error.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except (URLError, OSError) as error:
        return None, {}, str(error)
def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(data, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
def run(command):
    try:
        return subprocess.run(command, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=20).stdout
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        return ""

ASSISTANT
The sandbox has neither `ss` nor `netstat`, so section 6 printed a misleading "(none found)". Your machine runs 
Windows, where `netstat` is always present, but the script should never report "none" when it simply couldn't 
look. I'll make it say so explicitly, and fall back to `/proc/net/tcp` on Linux:
Tool Use: bash
command
python3 - <<'EOF'
p = "/tmp/diagnose_id.py"; s = open(p).read()

    pids = [line for line in run(["ss", "-ltnpH", f"sport = :{port}"]).splitlines() if line.strip()]
    print("\\n".join(pids) or "(none found)")
'''
new = '''else:
    pids = [line for line in run(["ss", "-ltnpH", f"sport = :{port}"]).splitlines() if line.strip()]
    if not pids and os.path.exists("/proc/net/tcp"):
        # Without ss, count listening sockets (state 0A) on the port; PIDs are not available.
        with open("/proc/net/tcp") as table:
            pids = [row for row in table.read().splitlines()[1:]
                    if row.split()[3] == "0A" and int(row.split()[1].split(":")[1], 16) == int(port)]

Function Results: bash
"""Read-only diagnosis for "id is missing from GET responses".
Run it from the project root with the interpreter and DATABASE_URL you use for the server:
    .venv\\Scripts\\python.exe diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]      (Windows)
    .venv/bin/python diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]                (Linux/macOS)
It only sends GET requests and reads the database in a read-only transaction.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import os
import platform
import subprocess
import sys
from decimal import Decimal
from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError
from urllib.parse import urlparse
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd())
BASE = (sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "http://localhost:8000").rstrip("/")
PRODUCTS = "/api/v1/products"
findings = []
def fetch(path):
    request = Request(BASE + path, headers={"Accept": "application/json"})
    try:
        with urlopen(request, timeout=10) as response:
            return response.status, response.headers, response.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except HTTPError as error:
        return error.code, error.headers, error.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except (URLError, OSError) as error:
        return None, {}, str(error)
def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(data, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
def run(command):
    try:
        return subprocess.run(command, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=20).stdout
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        return ""
print("== 1. Local code")
import inventory_management_service.main as main  # noqa: E402
source = open(main.__file__, encoding="utf-8").read()
print("python     :", sys.executable, platform.python_version())
print("main.py    :", main.__file__, "sha256", hashlib.sha256(source.encode()).hexdigest()[:12])
serializer_has_id = '"id": str(product.id)' in source
print("serializer emits id:", serializer_has_id)
if not serializer_has_id:
    findings.append("Your local main.py does not emit id: it differs from the reviewed code.")
print("git        :", run(["git", "log", "-1", "--format=%h %cd"]).strip() or "(git unavailable)")
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript

print("git changes:", changed or "(none)")
print("\n== 2. Local OpenAPI contract")
local_openapi = main.app.openapi()
local_fields = list(local_openapi["components"]["schemas"]["ProductResponse"]["properties"])
print("ProductResponse fields:", local_fields, "| digest", digest(local_openapi))
print("\n== 3. Running server at", BASE)
status, headers, body = fetch("/openapi.json")
served_openapi = None
if status is None:
    findings.append(f"Nothing answered at {BASE} ({body}). Is the server running on that address?")

    print("/openapi.json:", status, headers.get("server"), headers.get("content-type"))
    try:
        served_openapi = json.loads(body)
        served = served_openapi["components"]["schemas"].get("ProductResponse", {}).get("properties", 
{})
        print("served ProductResponse fields:", list(served), "| digest", digest(served_openapi))
        print("served GET routes:", sorted(p for p, ops in served_openapi["paths"].items() if "get" in 
ops))
        if served_openapi != local_openapi:
            findings.append("The server's OpenAPI differs from this code's: the process answering "
                            f"{BASE} is not running the files in {os.getcwd()}.")
    except (ValueError, KeyError) as error:
        findings.append(f"/openapi.json is not this API's document ({error!r}).")
print("\n== 4. Served GET responses (raw)")
served_item = None
if status is not None:
    status, headers, body = fetch(f"{PRODUCTS}?size=1")
    print(f"GET {PRODUCTS}?size=1 ->", status, headers.get("content-type"))
    print("   ", body[:400])
    try:
        items = json.loads(body, parse_float=Decimal)["items"]
        served_item = items[0] if items else None
    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
        findings.append("The list response is not the expected {items: [...]} envelope.")
    product_id = (served_item or {}).get("id") or "01a1b052-ec7c-76db-a862-e0da4baa5110"
    status, headers, body = fetch(f"{PRODUCTS}/{product_id}")
    print(f"GET {PRODUCTS}/{product_id} ->", status, headers.get("content-type"))
    print("   ", body[:400])
    try:
        single = json.loads(body, parse_float=Decimal)
        print("    members:", list(single))
        if status == 200 and "id" not in single:
            findings.append("The server really returns GET bodies without id.")
    except ValueError:
        single = None
    if served_item is not None and "id" not in served_item:
        findings.append("The server really returns list items without id.")

    from sqlalchemy import select, text
    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

ASSISTANT
I haven't found the cause of the missing `id`, and can't from here. The application code can't produce a product 
response without `id`, so the difference is on your desktop, and I've written a read-only script for you to run 
there. The test review is done: after removing redundant and brittle checks, all **304 tests pass against 
PostgreSQL 16** with none skipped. I made no application-code changes in this round.
## The missing `id`
Moving `id` to first position was a guess, and it didn't show why the field was missing. I haven't moved it back, 
because that would be another unsupported serialization change, and the tests no longer depend on member 
order either way.
**Facts from the code:**
- All three endpoints return a finished response that `product_data()` builds. FastAPI never re-serializes or filters it.
- `product_data()` has included `id` in every version that had GET endpoints. The original baseline had no GET 
endpoints at all.
- A test now starts a real uvicorn process, as you run it, and confirms over HTTP that it serves `id` and the same 
`/openapi.json` as the local code. Another test checks all 115 seeded products, in the list and via GET by ID.
**What's left** is that the response you saw came from something other than this code, or wasn't a product 
response:
- **A stale or different server.** uvicorn always sets `SO_REUSEADDR`, and on Windows that can let a new server 
start while an old one still holds port 8000. This is a hypothesis I couldn't test on Windows.
- **Another copy of the project**, or edits that weren't saved or reloaded.
- **An error response.** An old server returns 404/405 with no `id`, and this code's 404 for an unknown ID carries 
`productId` rather than `id`.
- **Looking at the list envelope** (`items`, `page`, …) instead of an item.
**The script** compares the local code, its OpenAPI schema, the running server's schema and raw responses, and 
the same database row serialized by the local code. It also lists every process on the port. It only sends GETs and 
reads in a read-only transaction. I tested it against three servers:
| Server | Verdict |
|---|---|
| This code | Code, schema and server agree; every response contains `id` |
| Another copy that omits `id` | Bodies lack `id`; this code returns `id` for the same row, so the process answering 
isn't your local files |
| Original baseline code | Schema differs; GET returns 405/404 problem documents |
In the second case the **OpenAPI schema still listed `id`**, so Swagger's schema view alone can't reveal this 
cause; only the raw response can.
Save the script below as `diagnose_id.py` in the project root. Start the server the way you normally do, then in 
PowerShell:
$env:DATABASE_URL = 'postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe diagnose_id.py http://localhost:8000

## Test review
**Kept, because they protect real requirements:**
- **Validation:** every validation, malformed-JSON and media-type case, the error list and count in `detail`, and 
"invalid input never reaches the service".
- **Errors:** exact 404/409/500 problem documents and `Location`.
- **Exact prices:** exact values, JSON numbers rather than strings, and the original "249.0000 → 249" output.
- **PostgreSQL behaviour:** filters checked against expectations computed from the sample data; paging for 
every sort and direction against independent SQL on data with real ties; soft deletion; UTC; seed preservation; 
migrations; concurrent inserts.
- **Database isolation:** a fresh schema per test, the dedicated-role checks, and the REPEATABLE READ snapshot.
**Changed, because they were brittle:**
- **JSON member order:** 9 checks now compare member sets or parsed values. Amount checks read the exact 
number text from the parser instead of matching `'"amount": 249,'`.
- **SQL text:** exact SQL strings, parameter names and SQLAlchemy's escape character are gone. The soft-delete 
and id-lookup checks use SQLAlchemy's structural `compare()`, and "count and page use the same criteria" 
compares the two `WHERE` clauses. Only the `id` tiebreaker check still reads SQL text, since SQLAlchemy has no 
public accessor for `ORDER BY`.
- **Call order:** exact event lists became properties (committed; one transaction; isolation set before the first 
query). Dropped: the "page query skipped" optimization check, an internal type check, and the exact log 
wording.
**Removed as redundant:** one API test duplicating the create/get/list comparison, and one search test 
duplicating `test_search["ring binder blue"]`.
## Mutation results (full suite against PostgreSQL)
| Injected change | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 16 real regressions: dropped `id`, price as string, price rounded, soft-deleted rows in list or get, no/wrong-
direction tiebreaker, unescaped wildcards, case-sensitive category, exclusive `maxPrice`, totals ignoring filters, 
search using one term, no snapshot, SKU limit +1, unvalidated `minPrice`, any unique violation → 409 | caught | 
all caught |
| 5 non-regressions: `id` moved back to 8th, reordered `WHERE` criteria, page-query optimization removed, 
equivalent count SQL, reworded log | pass | all pass |
The first run of the optimization mutation exposed one remaining coupling in my rewritten service tests. I fixed it, 
and the re-run passes. Without PostgreSQL, the unit tests alone still catch a dropped `id`, soft-deleted products 
in list or get, a missing tiebreaker, and totals that ignore filters. Filter semantics such as case-insensitive category 
need PostgreSQL.
Test counts: `test_api.py` 227, `test_service.py` 31, `test_postgres.py` 44, `test_startup.py` 2. The README's test 
descriptions are updated.

"""Read-only diagnosis for "id is missing from GET responses".
Run it from the project root with the interpreter and DATABASE_URL you use for the server:
    .venv\\Scripts\\python.exe diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]      (Windows)
    .venv/bin/python diagnose_id.py [http://localhost:8000]                (Linux/macOS)
It only sends GET requests and reads the database in a read-only transaction.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import os
import platform
import subprocess
import sys
from decimal import Decimal
from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError
from urllib.parse import urlparse
from urllib.request import Request, urlopen
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd())
BASE = (sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "http://localhost:8000").rstrip("/")
PRODUCTS = "/api/v1/products"
findings = []
def fetch(path):
    request = Request(BASE + path, headers={"Accept": "application/json"})
    try:
        with urlopen(request, timeout=10) as response:
            return response.status, response.headers, response.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except HTTPError as error:
        return error.code, error.headers, error.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
    except (URLError, OSError) as error:
        return None, {}, str(error)
def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(data, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]
def run(command):
    try:
        return subprocess.run(command, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=20).stdout
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        return ""
print("== 1. Local code")
import inventory_management_service.main as main  # noqa: E402
source = open(main.__file__, encoding="utf-8").read()
print("python     :", sys.executable, platform.python_version())
print("main.py    :", main.__file__, "sha256", hashlib.sha256(source.encode()).hexdigest()[:12])

by_file = {}
for node in gone:
    by_file.setdefault(node.split("::")[0], []).append(node)
for f, nodes in by_file.items():
    print(f"\n{f}: {len(nodes)} ids no longer collected")
    names = {}
    for n in nodes:
        base = n.split("::")[1].split("[")[0]
        names[base] = names.get(base, 0) + 1
    for base, c in sorted(names.items()):
        print(f"   {c:3}  {base}")

"test_category_filter", "test_list_blank_text_filters_are_ignored"]:

    for n in current[:2]: print("    current:", repr(n.split("::")[1])[:90])

--- PAGE 276 ---
ASSISTANT
Found it, and it's a bug in my own analysis, not in the tests. In that last script I read `now.txt` with `.split()`, which 
splits on every whitespace character. Parametrized IDs containing spaces, such as `test_search[ring binder blue]` 
or `test_create_rejects_invalid_values[{"sku": "A B"}...]`, were broken into fragments and then failed to match. The 
earlier script used `split("\n")` correctly. Re-running the classification with line-based parsing:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json, collections
cached = set(json.load(open(".pytest_cache/v/cache/nodeids")))
now = set(open("/tmp/now.txt").read().splitlines()) - {""}
assert len(now) == 304, len(now)
base = lambda n: n.split("[")[0]
round1 = {"tests/test_api.py::" + n for n in ["test_attributes_required", "test_create_contract",
          "test_get_product_emits_exact_json_number", "test_invalid_fields", "test_malformed", 
"test_unsupported_media"]}
removed = sorted(n for n in cached - now if base(n) not in round1)
renames = {
 "tests/test_api.py::test_id_is_present_and_first": 
"tests/test_api.py::test_id_is_present_and_identifies_the_product",
 "tests/test_api.py::test_openapi_product_schema_lists_id_first": 
"tests/test_api.py::test_openapi_product_schema_documents_id",
 "tests/test_service.py::test_list_orders_by_sort_key_then_id": 
"tests/test_service.py::test_list_breaks_ties_by_id_in_the_sort_direction",
 "tests/test_service.py::test_list_uses_one_repeatable_read_snapshot": 
"tests/test_service.py::test_list_reads_count_and_page_from_one_snapshot",
 "tests/test_service.py::test_get_selects_one_non_deleted_product_by_id": 
"tests/test_service.py::test_get_looks_up_the_requested_id_and_excludes_deleted_products",
}
added_names = {"tests/test_service.py::test_list_count_and_page_use_identical_criteria",
               "tests/test_postgres.py::test_every_seeded_product_is_served_with_its_id",
               "tests/test_postgres.py::test_running_server_serves_ids_and_this_codes_openapi"} | 
set(renames.values())
added = sorted(n for n in now if base(n) in added_names)
r = collections.Counter(base(i) for i in removed); a = collections.Counter(base(i) for i in added)
print(f"removed this round: {len(removed)}   added this round: {len(added)}   net: {len(added) - 
len(removed)}\n")
print("RENAMED (same requirement; assertions made format-independent):")
for old, new in renames.items():
    print(f"  {r[old]:2} -> {a[new]:2}  {old.split('::')[1]}  ->  {new.split('::')[1]}")
print("\nREMOVED with no same-named successor:")
for name, c in sorted(r.items()):
    if name not in renames: print(f"  {c:2}  {name}")
print("\nNEW:")
for name, c in sorted(a.items()):
    if name not in renames.values(): print(f"  {c:2}  {name}")
files = lambda ids: collections.Counter(n.split("::")[0] for n in ids)
net = files(added); net.subtract(files(removed))
print("\nnet per file:", {k.split('/')[1]: v for k, v in net.items()}, "| 316 + net =", 316 + 
len(added) - len(removed))
EOF

--- PAGE 283 ---
## The trade-off
Offset arithmetic, wildcard escaping, multi-word search and filter behaviour used to be checked twice: by SQL-
text tests that need no database, and by PostgreSQL tests. Now only the PostgreSQL tests check them. So if 
those tests are skipped again, as they were on your desktop before the `.env` fix, those regressions would go 
unnoticed. The SQL-independent unit tests still catch a missing `id`, soft-deleted products appearing, a missing 
tiebreaker, and totals that ignore the filters.
If you'd rather keep a no-database check for any of the removed behaviours, I can add one back. Of those, offset 
arithmetic would need a check on SQLAlchemy internals; the others would be re-tied to SQL text, which the 
review set out to remove.
HUMAN
Make the existing full-test workflow reproducible and ensure it cannot report success when PostgreSQL 
integration tests were skipped. Do not add application functionality, change the API contract, or restore brittle 
SQL-formatting assertions.
Add a documented verification command or small script that:
Validates the dedicated test database configuration before running tests.
Runs the complete suite against PostgreSQL.
Exits unsuccessfully if the required database is unavailable, configuration is missing or unsafe, tests fail, or 
PostgreSQL integration tests are skipped.
Preserves the existing option to run database-independent unit tests separately.
Does not modify the development database or terminate unrelated processes.
Verify the workflow with valid configuration, missing configuration, an unreachable database, and a 
development-database URL supplied accidentally. Show the exit codes and relevant output. Confirm that test 
schemas and any processes created by verification are cleaned up.
Keep the existing project structure and update the README with commands I can reproduce. Do not optimize for 
a particular test count or coverage percentage.

ASSISTANT
## Plan
Before writing code, here's what I intend, so you can object early.
**Where it lives.** A pytest option, `--require-postgres`, implemented in `tests/conftest.py`, so no new files or 
folders. The documented command is `python -m pytest --require-postgres`. Plain `pytest` and `pytest -m "not 
postgres"` behave exactly as today.
**Checks before any test runs.** Each failure exits with code **4**:
- The test URL is set, either as an environment variable or in `.env`.
- It is a valid URL using the `postgresql+psycopg` driver, with database and role both named `inventra_test`.
- `DATABASE_URL`, if set, doesn't name the test database.
- PostgreSQL is reachable within 5 seconds.
- The server itself confirms the database and user are `inventra_test`, the role is NOSUPERUSER and 
NOCREATEDB, and it has CREATE on the database.
- No test paths are given, because this run is meant to be the complete suite.
**Safety during the run.** `DATABASE_URL` is removed from the pytest process after the checks, so no test can 
reach the development database through the app's engine. The run creates only per-test `test_<random>` 
schemas, and it never kills anything except the one server process it starts itself.
**Checks after the run.** Any failure exits with code **1**:
- Any test failed.
- Any PostgreSQL test was skipped.
- No PostgreSQL test ran.
- Any test was deselected (by `-k`, `-m` or `--lf`).
- Any schema this run created still exists.
I'll start with the existing fixture and helper code I'll be hooking into:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && sed -n 1,20p tests/conftest.py; grep -n "def 
setting\|def fake_session\|^def database\|pytest.skip\|CREATE SCHEMA\|def payload" tests/conftest.py; 
.venv/bin/python -c "import pytest; from _pytest.config import Config; print(pytest.__version__, 
list(Config.ArgsSource))"

# Full verification: pytest --require-postgres
#
# A normal run skips the PostgreSQL tests when no test database is configured.
# With --require-postgres, the run validates the dedicated test database before any
# test starts and fails instead of reporting success if a PostgreSQL test was skipped
# or deselected, none ran, or a test schema was left behind.
# Exit codes: 0 passed, 1 tests failed or verification incomplete, 4 configuration
# missing, unsafe, or database unreachable.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Verification:
    def __init__(self, url, server):
        self.url = url                  # validated test database URL
        self.server = server            # description shown in the report header
        self.postgres_tests = set()     # node ids of the collected PostgreSQL tests
        self.postgres_ran = 0
        self.postgres_skipped = []
        self.deselected = 0
        self.schemas = set()            # schemas created by the database fixture

def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--require-postgres", action="store_true",
        help="Full verification: validate the dedicated PostgreSQL test database first, "
             "then fail unless every test, including every PostgreSQL test, ran and passed.",
    )
def refuse(message):
    """Stop before any test runs; pytest exits with code 4 (usage error)."""
    pytest.exit(f"Full verification refused: {message}", returncode=pytest.ExitCode.USAGE_ERROR)
def validate_test_database(url_text):
    """Check that the URL names the dedicated test database and that it is reachable
    and safe. Return (url, description). Nothing is created or changed."""
    if not url_text:
        refuse("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is not set in the environment or the project's .env file.")
    try:
        url = make_url(url_text)
    except ArgumentError:
        refuse("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL is not a valid database URL.")
    shown = url.render_as_string(hide_password=True)
    if (url.drivername, url.database, url.username) != ("postgresql+psycopg", TEST_DATABASE, 
TEST_DATABASE):
        refuse(f"{shown} is not the dedicated test database. Use the postgresql+psycopg driver "
               f"with a database and role both named {TEST_DATABASE}.")
    development = os.environ.get("DATABASE_URL")
    if development:
        try:
            development_database = make_url(development).database
        except ArgumentError:
            development_database = None
        if development_database == TEST_DATABASE:
            refuse(f"DATABASE_URL also names {TEST_DATABASE}; the application and the tests "
                   "must not share a database.")
    engine = create_engine(url, poolclass=NullPool, connect_args={"connect_timeout": 5})
    try:
        with engine.connect() as connection:
            # Ask the server itself, in case a proxy or service file maps the name elsewhere.
            facts = connection.execute(text("""
                SELECT current_database() AS database, current_user AS role,
                       rolsuper OR rolcreatedb AS privileged,
                       has_database_privilege(current_database(), 'CREATE') AS can_create,
                       current_setting('server_version') AS version
                FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user
            """)).one()
    except DBAPIError as error:
        reason = str(error.orig).strip().splitlines()[0] if error.orig else str(error)
        refuse(f"cannot connect to {shown}: {reason}")
    finally:
        engine.dispose()
    if (facts.database, facts.role) != (TEST_DATABASE, TEST_DATABASE):
        refuse(f"the server reports database {facts.database!r} and role {facts.role!r}, "
               f"not {TEST_DATABASE}.")
    if facts.privileged:
        refuse(f"role {TEST_DATABASE} must be NOSUPERUSER and NOCREATEDB.")
    if not facts.can_create:
        refuse(f"role {TEST_DATABASE} cannot create schemas; run tests/init-test-db.sql.")
    return url, f"PostgreSQL {facts.version} at {url.host}:{url.port or 5432}/{facts.database} as 
{facts.role}"

def pytest_sessionstart(session):
    global VERIFICATION
    config = session.config
    if not config.getoption("--require-postgres"):
        return
    if config.args_source != pytest.Config.ArgsSource.TESTPATHS:
        refuse("it runs the complete suite; do not pass test paths.")
    url, server = validate_test_database(setting("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL"))
    # No test needs the development database. Removing DATABASE_URL from this process
    # ensures nothing can reach it through the application's engine.
    os.environ.pop("DATABASE_URL", None)
    VERIFICATION = Verification(url, server)
def pytest_report_header(config):
    if VERIFICATION is not None:
        return f"full verification: {VERIFICATION.server}"
def pytest_deselected(items):
    if VERIFICATION is not None:
        VERIFICATION.deselected += len(items)
def pytest_collection_finish(session):
    if VERIFICATION is not None:
        VERIFICATION.postgres_tests = {
            item.nodeid for item in session.items if item.get_closest_marker("postgres")
        }
def pytest_runtest_logreport(report):
    if VERIFICATION is None or report.nodeid not in VERIFICATION.postgres_tests:
        return
    if report.skipped:
        VERIFICATION.postgres_skipped.append(report.nodeid)
    elif report.when == "call":
        VERIFICATION.postgres_ran += 1
def pytest_sessionfinish(session, exitstatus):
    if VERIFICATION is None or exitstatus not in (pytest.ExitCode.OK, pytest.ExitCode.TESTS_FAILED):
        return
    problems = VERIFICATION.problems
    if VERIFICATION.postgres_skipped:
        problems.append(f"{len(VERIFICATION.postgres_skipped)} PostgreSQL integration test(s) were 
skipped: "
                        + ", ".join(VERIFICATION.postgres_skipped[:5]))
    if VERIFICATION.postgres_ran == 0:
        problems.append("no PostgreSQL integration test ran.")
    if VERIFICATION.deselected:
        problems.append(f"{VERIFICATION.deselected} test(s) were deselected (-k, -m, or --lf); "
                        "the full verification runs every test.")
    if VERIFICATION.schemas:
        engine = create_engine(VERIFICATION.url, poolclass=NullPool, connect_args={"connect_timeout": 
5})
        try:
            with engine.connect() as connection:
                leftover = connection.scalars(

def database():
    url = setting("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL")
    if not url and VERIFICATION is not None:
        pytest.fail("INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL disappeared during full verification")
    if not url:
        pytest.skip(
            "Set INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL, as an environment variable or in the project's "
            ".env file, to run PostgreSQL integration tests"
        )
    url = make_url(url)
    if (
        url.drivername != "postgresql+psycopg"
        or url.database != TEST_DATABASE
        or url.username != TEST_DATABASE
    ):
        pytest.fail("Use a dedicated PostgreSQL database and role both named inventra_test")
    schema = f"test_{uuid4().hex}"
    admin = create_engine(url)
    engine = create_engine(url, connect_args={"options": f"-c search_path={schema}"})
    try:
        with admin.begin() as connection:
            privileged = connection.scalar(text("""
                SELECT rolsuper OR rolcreatedb FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user
            """))
            if privileged:
                pytest.fail("The test role must be NOCREATEDB and NOSUPERUSER")
            connection.execute(text(f'CREATE SCHEMA "{schema}"'))
        if VERIFICATION is not None:
            VERIFICATION.schemas.add(schema)
        try:
            config = Config(str(ROOT / "alembic.ini"))
            with engine.connect() as connection:
                config.attributes["connection"] = connection
                command.upgrade(config, "head")
            yield engine, config
        finally:
            engine.dispose()
            with admin.begin() as connection:
                connection.execute(text(f'DROP SCHEMA "{schema}" CASCADE'))
    finally:
        admin.dispose()

ASSISTANT
The tracking line runs right after `CREATE SCHEMA` commits, which is correct.
Now the verification harness. Before the scenarios I'll record:
- **A fingerprint of the development database:** row count, an MD5 of every product row, the Alembic version

- **The test database's `test_*` schema count.**
- **The process table.**
I'll also start **two unrelated processes** that must survive every scenario: a development server on port 8000, as

--- PAGE 295 ---
ASSISTANT
Both refuse with exit 4, and the password is masked (`***`). The second variant didn't test what I intended, 
though. Something in this sandbox's network answered at `10.255.255.1` ("invalid response to SSL negotiation: H" 
looks like an HTTP proxy), so the timeout path never ran. To really exercise the timeout, I'll start a local listener 
that accepts connections and never replies, and stop it afterwards:
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && export DEV_PID=$(cat /tmp/dev.pid) SLEEP_PID=$(cat 
/tmp/sleep.pid); source /tmp/state.sh
export DATABASE_URL='postgresql+psycopg://inventra:inventra_dev@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra?
connect_timeout=5'
python3 -c "
import socket, time
s = socket.socket(); s.setsockopt(socket.SOL_SOCKET, socket.SO_REUSEADDR, 1); s.bind(('127.0.0.1', 
5440)); s.listen()
held = []
while True:
    c, _ = s.accept(); held.append(c)   # accept, never answer
" & SILENT=$!; sleep 0.5
start=$(date +%s.%N)
INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL="postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@127.0.0.1:5440/inventra_t
est" \
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest --require-postgres -q -p no:warnings > /tmp/s3b.log 2>&1; code=$?
echo "EXIT CODE: $code  (took $(echo "$(date +%s.%N) - $start" | bc | cut -c1-4)s)"; head -1 
/tmp/s3b.log
kill $SILENT; wait $SILENT 2>/dev/null; kill -0 $SILENT 2>/dev/null && echo "silent listener still 
running" || echo "silent listener (created for this check) stopped"
echo "-- after:"; state

**Scenario 4: a development-database URL supplied by accident**, in four variants:
- (a) the development URL exported as the test URL;
- (b) the same mistake made in `.env`, the PyCharm route;

- (d) a correct-looking URL whose role has been given CREATEDB, to exercise the server-side privilege check. I'll

Now the central guarantee: **a run that gets past configuration still can't report success when the suite was 
incomplete.** To inject problems without editing any project file, I'll use small throwaway pytest plugins in `/tmp`, 
loaded with `-p`:
| Case | Injected problem |
|---|---|
| 5 | One PostgreSQL test is skipped at runtime |
| 6 | An ordinary test fails |
| 7 | A test schema is left behind after teardown |
| 8 | The PostgreSQL tests are deselected with `-m "not postgres"` |
| 9 | Tests are deselected with `-k` |
| 10 | An explicit test path is passed |
Tool Use: bash
command
mkdir -p /tmp/plugins && cat > /tmp/plugins/skip_one_postgres.py <<'EOF'
import pytest
@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)
def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
    if item.name == "test_currency_filter":
        pytest.skip("simulated: database feature unavailable")
EOF
cat > /tmp/plugins/fail_one_unit.py <<'EOF'
import pytest
@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)
def pytest_runtest_call(item):
    if item.name == "test_list_defaults_and_envelope":
        raise AssertionError("simulated regression")
EOF
cat > /tmp/plugins/leave_schema.py <<'EOF'
# After the fixture has dropped its schema, re-create it to simulate a cleanup failure.
import sys
import pytest
from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text
@pytest.hookimpl(hookwrapper=True)
def pytest_runtest_teardown(item):
    yield
    if item.name == "test_currency_filter":
        verification = sys.modules["conftest"].VERIFICATION
        schema = sorted(verification.schemas)[-1] if verification.schemas else None
        engine = create_engine(verification.url)
        with engine.begin() as connection:
            for name in verification.schemas:      # the schema this test used is among them
                if not connection.scalar(text("SELECT count(*) FROM pg_namespace WHERE nspname = :n"), 
{"n": name}):
                    connection.execute(text(f'CREATE SCHEMA "{name}"')); break
        engine.dispose()
EOF
echo plugins written
Tool Use: bash
command

--- PAGE 304 ---
Run all tests against that database:
```powershell
$env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL = 
'postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_test'
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q
Remove-Item Env:INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL
```
The PostgreSQL tests cover Alembic upgrade, downgrade and re-upgrade, committed rows, exact prices, 
normalized duplicate rejection, and concurrent inserts. For reads, they cover create-then-get round 
trips, hiding soft-deleted products, gap-free and duplicate-free paging for every sort field and 
direction over the seeded catalog, search (including literal `%`, `_` and `\`), category, price and 
currency filters checked against `data/sample-products.json`, and UTC output under a non-UTC session 
time zone. Each test migrates a fresh schema and drops only that schema at cleanup. The fixture 
requires an explicit test URL and a dedicated `inventra_test` database and role. The role has 
permission to create schemas in that database and has neither superuser nor CREATEDB privileges. 
Without the test URL, pytest reports the PostgreSQL tests as skipped.
IDE test runners such as PyCharm do not inherit variables set in a terminal. Instead of setting 
`INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` in each run configuration, you can put it in a `.env` file in the project 
root. The file is git-ignored, and a variable set in the environment takes precedence:
```
INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@localhost:5432/inventra_te
st
```
Only the tests read `.env`; the application still needs `DATABASE_URL` in its environment. A 
configured database failure fails the tests.
## Extension points
- **Read indexes.** The list queries do not add indexes; the sample catalog is small, and each list 
request counts all matching rows. For large catalogs, add a new Alembic revision with partial indexes 
`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` on the sort keys plus `id`, for example `(name, id)`, and on 
`lower(category)`. Substring search needs a `pg_trgm` GIN index to avoid sequential scans. For deep 
paging, consider keyset (cursor) pagination on the same `(sort key, id)` order.
- **Update** can reuse the mapped version column for stale-write detection. Define the request and 
concurrency contract when implementing the route.
- **Soft delete** can use the nullable `deleted_at` column and the existing status consistency checks. 
Retain the SKU reservation. Get and List already exclude rows with `deleted_at` set.
- **Bulk create and bulk delete** can use separate request schemas and service operations. The current 
service owns the single-create transaction. Extract transaction-neutral insertion logic before 
implementing atomic batches.
The registered product operations are POST, GET by ID, and GET list. The project separates HTTP 
handling and Pydantic schemas (`main.py`, `schemas.py`) from the transactions and queries 
(`service.py`) and the ORM model.

This is the command to use when a passing result must mean that the PostgreSQL integration tests ran. 
A plain `pytest` run still skips them when no test database is configured and still exits 0, as 
before. Before any test starts, `--require-postgres` checks the configuration, read from the 
environment or `.env`, and refuses to run if:
- `INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is missing or is not a valid URL;
- the URL does not use `postgresql+psycopg` with a database and role both named `inventra_test` (an 
11/6/26, 11:31 PM live-compare-d669c4bf__cont12-4c5ba0d4 — transcript
about:blank 305/393

--- PAGE 306 ---
accidentally supplied development URL is rejected here);
- `DATABASE_URL` also names `inventra_test`;
- PostgreSQL does not answer within 5 seconds;
- the server reports a different database or role, the role has SUPERUSER or CREATEDB, or it lacks 
CREATE on the database;
- test paths are given (the verification always runs the complete suite).
After the run, it fails if any test failed, any PostgreSQL test was skipped, no PostgreSQL test ran, 
any test was deselected (`-k`, `-m`, `--lf`), or a test schema it created still exists. A summary line 
reports the outcome.
| Exit code | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | Every test ran and passed, including every PostgreSQL integration test, and all test schemas 
were removed. |
| 1 | A test failed, or the verification was incomplete (skipped or deselected tests, or leftover 
schemas). |
| 4 | Refused before running: configuration missing, invalid, or unsafe, or the database is 
unreachable. |
The verification touches only the test database: each PostgreSQL test creates its own `test_<random>` 
schema and drops it. `DATABASE_URL` is removed from the test process after the checks, so no test can 
reach the development database through the application. One test starts the application with uvicorn 
on a free port, pointed at its test schema, and stops that process itself. A server you have running 
on port 8000 is not affected.

ASSISTANT
My README line about PyCharm exposed a real flaw in my own rule. PyCharm's test runner passes the selected 
target, such as the `tests` folder, as an explicit argument. "No test paths allowed" would therefore refuse even a 
full-suite run from PyCharm, or a plain `pytest --require-postgres tests`. The rule should be about **what gets 
collected**, not how it was requested. I'll replace it:
- Refuse node IDs (`file::test`).
- After collection and before any test runs, refuse unless every `tests/test_*.py` module was collected.
Combined with the existing deselection check, any way of naming the complete suite is accepted and any partial 
run is refused.
Tool Use: bash
command
cd /workspace/inventory-management-service && python3 - <<'EOF'
p = "tests/conftest.py"; s = open(p).read()
def rep(old, new):
    global s
    assert s.count(old) == 1, old
    s = s.replace(old, new)
rep('''    if config.args_source != pytest.Config.ArgsSource.TESTPATHS:
        refuse("it runs the complete suite; do not pass test paths.")
''', '''    if any("::" in argument for argument in config.args):
        refuse("it runs the complete suite; do not select individual tests.")
''')
rep('''def pytest_collection_finish(session):
    if VERIFICATION is not None:
        VERIFICATION.postgres_tests = {
            item.nodeid for item in session.items if item.get_closest_marker("postgres")
        }
''', '''def pytest_collection_finish(session):
    if VERIFICATION is None:
        return
    # However the suite was named (no arguments, the project, or the tests folder, as
    # IDE runners do), every test module must have been collected.
    expected = {path.name for path in (ROOT / "tests").glob("test_*.py")}
    collected = {item.path.name for item in session.items}
    if missing := sorted(expected - collected):
        refuse(f"it runs the complete suite, but these test modules were not collected: {', 
'.join(missing)}.")
    VERIFICATION.postgres_tests = {
        item.nodeid for item in session.items if item.get_closest_marker("postgres")
    }
''')
open(p, "w").write(s)
p = "README.md"; s = open(p).read()
rep('''- test paths are given (the verification always runs the complete suite).''',
    '''- individual tests are selected, or a test module is not collected (the verification always 
runs the complete suite; naming the project or the `tests` folder is fine).''')
rep('''add `--require-postgres` to the pytest run configuration's *Additional Arguments* and leave the 
target set to the whole project.''',
    '''add `--require-postgres` to the pytest run configuration's *Additional Arguments*, with the 
project or the `tests` folder as the target.''')
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF
PYTHONPATH=/tmp/pyflakes .venv/bin/python -m pyflakes tests/conftest.py && echo pyflakes clean

  label=$1 expected=$2; shift 2; envs=(); while [ "$1" != "--" ]; do envs+=("$1"); shift; done; shift
  env -u INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL DATABASE_URL="$DEV" "${envs[@]}" $PY -m pytest --require-postgres

--- PAGE 323 ---
ASSISTANT
The full-suite workflow is now one command that only succeeds when every PostgreSQL integration test actually 
ran and passed. I checked all 17 scenarios against the final code, and every exit code matched the 
documentation. This round changed only `tests/conftest.py` and `README.md`; no application code or API 
behaviour changed.
INVENTRA_TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgresql+psycopg://inventra_test:inventra_test@127.0.0.1:5432/inventra_te
st' \
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest --require-postgres
On Windows, use the same `--require-postgres` flag (PowerShell version in the README). The test URL can also 
come from `.env`. Plain `pytest` and the unit-test-only `pytest -m "not postgres"` behave as before: with no test 
database configured, the unit-only run exited 0 with 260 passed.
## What the command does
- **Before any test runs (exit 4 if refused):**
  - The test URL must exist, be valid, use the `postgresql+psycopg` driver, and name a database and role that are 
both `inventra_test`.
  - `DATABASE_URL` must not name the test database.
  - PostgreSQL must answer within 5 seconds.
  - The server itself must confirm the database and role, that the role is not a superuser and cannot create 
databases, and that it can create schemas.
  - The whole suite must be selected. Naming the project or the `tests` folder is fine, as IDE runners do; selecting 
single tests, single modules, or filtering with `-k`, `-m` or `--lf` is refused.
- **During the run:** `DATABASE_URL` is removed from the test process, so no test can reach the development 
database. The run creates only per-test `test_<random>` schemas. The only process it starts is one uvicorn server 
on a free port, which it stops itself.
- **After the run (exit 1):** any failed test, any skipped PostgreSQL test, no PostgreSQL tests at all, or any schema 
this run created that still exists.
## Verification results
To simulate skips, failures and leftover schemas without editing project files, I used small throwaway pytest 
plugins kept in `/tmp`.

| 1b | Valid, with `tests` folder passed as an IDE would | **0** | same |

database` |

**Cleanup and safety:**
- The development database (126 products, an MD5 over every row, migration version, schema list) was identical 
before and after all scenarios.
- `inventra_test` has no leftover test schemas.
- An unrelated development server on port 8000 and an unrelated `sleep` process stayed alive throughout.
- No pytest or uvicorn processes remained after any run.
- The role change from 4d was reverted, and no `.env` file was left behind.
- I then stopped only the two stand-in processes I had started, by PID, and deleted the temporary files.
- The README's Linux command, run exactly as written, printed `0`.
**Fixes made along the way:**
- My first rule forbade any test path. That would have refused a full run from PyCharm, which passes the `tests` 
folder. It now checks instead that every test module was collected.
- Deselection is now refused up front with the correct reason; before, it was misreported as missing modules.
- A refused run no longer prints a misleading "tests failed" line.
## Not verified
- PowerShell commands and the PyCharm setup (adding `--require-postgres` under the run configuration's 
Additional Arguments); I only simulated PyCharm passing the `tests` folder.
- A genuinely silent remote host. My test address was answered by something in the sandbox network, so I 
tested the timeout with a local listener instead.
- Behaviour when a run is killed mid-way: pytest can't clean up then, and leftover `test_*` schemas would need 
dropping by hand.
- PostgreSQL 18; everything here ran on PostgreSQL 16.

def run_migrations():
    """Apply pending migrations before serving requests, one process at a time."""
    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    config = Config(str(root / "alembic.ini"))
    with engine().begin() as connection:
        # Concurrent application workers must not migrate the database together.
        connection.execute(text("SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(746238190517)"))
        config.attributes["connection"] = connection
        command.upgrade(config, "head")
